States that lead neither to accumulation nor to reduction are: Results of wholesome and unwholesome phenomena of the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and supramundane; the feeling aggregate, perception aggregate, mental formations aggregate, and consciousness aggregate; functional states that are neither wholesome nor unwholesome nor kamma-results; all material phenomena; and the unconditioned element.
^1-1022

States of trainees are: The four supramundane paths, and the three lower fruits of spiritual attainment.
^1-1023

States of perfected ones are: The highest fruit, Arahantship.
^1-1024

States that are neither of trainees nor of perfected ones are: Excluding those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena of the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and immaterial sphere; the feeling aggregate, perception aggregate, mental formations aggregate, and consciousness aggregate; all material phenomena; and the unconditioned element.
^1-1025

Limited states are: All wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena of the sense sphere; the matter aggregate, feeling aggregate, perception aggregate, mental formations aggregate, and consciousness aggregate.
^1-1026

Sublime states are: Wholesome and indeterminate phenomena of the fine-material and immaterial spheres; the feeling aggregate, perception aggregate, mental formations aggregate, and consciousness aggregate.
^1-1027

Illimitable states are: The supramundane paths, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element.
^1-1028

States with limited objects are: Those consciousness and mental factors that arise taking limited states as their object.
^1-1029

Which are the states that have a sublime object? Mental states and mental factors that arise taking sublime phenomena as their object are states with a sublime object.
^1-1030

Which are the states that have an immeasurable object? Mental states and mental factors that arise taking immeasurable phenomena as their object are states with an immeasurable object.
^1-1031

Which are the inferior states? The three unwholesome roots—greed, hatred, delusion—and associated defilements; the feeling aggregate up to the consciousness aggregate associated with them; and bodily, verbal, and mental kamma originating from them are inferior states.
^1-1032

Which are the middling states? Wholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, belonging to the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and immaterial sphere; and the form aggregate up to the consciousness aggregate are middling states.
^1-1033

Which are the sublime states? The unincluded paths, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element (Nibbāna) are sublime states.
^1-1034

Which are the states fixed in wrongness? The five heinous actions and wrong view fixed in certainty are states fixed in wrongness.
^1-1035

Which are the states fixed in rightness? The four unincluded paths are states fixed in rightness.
^1-1036

Which are the indeterminate states? Except for those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate states belonging to the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and unincluded; the feeling aggregate up to the consciousness aggregate; all matter; and the unconditioned element (Nibbāna) are indeterminate states.
^1-1037

Which are the states that have the Noble Path as their object? Mental states and mental factors that arise taking the Noble Path as their object are states with the Noble Path as their object.
^1-1038

Which are the states rooted in the Path? For one endowed with the Noble Path, the feeling aggregate up to the consciousness aggregate associated with the path factors (excluding the path factors themselves) are states rooted in the Path. For one endowed with the Noble Path, right view is both path and root; the feeling aggregate up to the consciousness aggregate associated with it (excluding right view itself) are states rooted in the Path. For one endowed with the Noble Path, non-greed, non-hatred, and non-delusion are the roots of the Path; the feeling aggregate up to the consciousness aggregate associated with them are states rooted in the Path.
^1-1039

Which are the dhammas that have the path as their dominant condition? Those consciousness and mental factors that arise making the Noble Path predominant. These dhammas have the path as their dominant condition. For one endowed with the Noble Path, cultivating the path with investigation as predominance, excluding investigation; the associated feeling aggregate...pe...consciousness aggregate. These dhammas have the path as their dominant condition.
^1-1040

Which are the arisen dhammas?
Those dhammas that are born, become, arisen, produced, manifested, appeared, arisen, come to pass, supervened, caused to arise, and are included in the category of arisen: form, feeling, perception, mental formations, consciousness. These dhammas are arisen.
^1-1041

Which are the not arisen dhammas? Those dhammas that are not born, not become, not arisen, not produced, not manifested, not appeared, not arisen, not come to pass, not supervened, not caused to arise, and are included in the category of not arisen: form, feeling, perception, mental formations, consciousness. These dhammas are not arisen.
^1-1042

Which are the dhammas capable of arising? The unripened results of wholesome and unwholesome dhammas, which are resultant, belonging to the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and not included in these; the feeling aggregate...pe...consciousness aggregate; and also the form that will arise due to kamma. These dhammas are capable of arising.
^1-1043

Which are the past dhammas? Those dhammas that are past, ceased, gone, changed, set, completely set, having arisen and then gone, past, and are included in the category of past: form, feeling, perception, mental formations, consciousness. These dhammas are past.
^1-1044

Which are the future dhammas? Those dhammas that are not born, not become, not arisen, not produced, not manifested, not appeared, not arisen, not come to pass, not supervened, not caused to arise, future, and are included in the category of future: form, feeling, perception, mental formations, consciousness. These dhammas are future.
^1-1045

Which are the present dhammas?
Those dhammas that are born, become, arisen, produced, manifested, appeared, arisen, come to pass, supervened, caused to arise, present, and are included in the category of present: form, feeling, perception, mental formations, consciousness. These dhammas are present.
^1-1046

Which are the dhammas with past objects?
Those consciousness and mental factors that arise taking past dhammas as their object. These dhammas have past objects.
^1-1047

Which are the dhammas with future objects?
Those consciousness and mental factors that arise taking future dhammas as their object. These dhammas have future objects.
^1-1048

Which are the dhammas with present objects?
Those consciousness and mental factors that arise taking present dhammas as their object. These dhammas have present objects.
^1-1049

What are internal phenomena?
Those phenomena of those beings that are internal, personal, fixed, pertaining to the individual, and appropriated: form, feeling, perception, mental formations, consciousness – these phenomena are internal.
^1-1050

What are external phenomena?
Those phenomena of other beings, other individuals, that are internal, personal, fixed, pertaining to the individual, and appropriated: form, feeling, perception, mental formations, consciousness – these phenomena are external.
^1-1051

What are internal and external phenomena?
Both of these – these phenomena are internal and external.
^1-1052

What are phenomena with internal objects?
Those consciousness and mental factors that arise taking internal phenomena as object – these phenomena have internal objects.
^1-1053

What are phenomena with external objects?
Those consciousness and mental factors that arise taking external phenomena as object – these phenomena have external objects.
^1-1054

What are phenomena with internal and external objects?
Those consciousness and mental factors that arise taking internal and external phenomena as object – these phenomena have internal and external objects.
^1-1055

What are visible and impinging phenomena?
The visible object – these phenomena are visible and impinging.
^1-1056

What are invisible and impinging phenomena?
The eye-base, ear-base, nose-base, tongue-base, body-base, sound-base, smell-base, taste-base, tangible-base – these phenomena are invisible and impinging.
^1-1057

What are invisible and non-impinging phenomena?
The feeling aggregate, perception aggregate, mental formations aggregate, consciousness aggregate; and that matter which is invisible and non-impinging, included in the dhamma-base; and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are invisible and non-impinging.
The Triad.
^1-1058

#### Exposition of Dyads ^1-3-2-0

##### Cluster of Roots ^1-3-2-1-0

Which states are roots? Three wholesome roots, three unwholesome roots, three indeterminate roots, nine sense-sphere roots, six fine-material sphere roots, six immaterial sphere roots, six supramundane roots.
^1-1059

Among these, which are the three wholesome roots? Non-greed, non-hatred, non-delusion.
^1-1060

Among these, what is non-greed?
That which is non-greed, not being greedy, the state of not being greedy, non-attachment, not being attached, the state of not being attached, non-covetousness, non-greed, a wholesome root—this is called non-greed.
^1-1061

Among these, what is non-hatred?
That which is non-hatred, not being hateful, the state of not being hateful, loving-kindness, being loving, the state of being loving, sympathy, being sympathetic, the state of being sympathetic, wishing for welfare, compassion, non-ill-will, not being ill-willed, non-hatred, a wholesome root—this is called non-hatred.
^1-1062

Among these, what is non-delusion?
Knowledge of suffering, knowledge of the origin of suffering, knowledge of the cessation of suffering, knowledge of the path leading to the cessation of suffering, knowledge of the past, knowledge of the future, knowledge of both past and future, knowledge of dependent arising in conditionally arisen states;
that which is such wisdom, understanding, investigation, thorough investigation, investigation of phenomena, discernment, observation, close observation, sagacity, skill, proficiency, analysis, thought, examination, insight, intelligence, leading wisdom, insight, clear comprehension, goad, wisdom, wisdom faculty, power of wisdom, wisdom-weapon, wisdom-palace, wisdom-light, wisdom-radiance, wisdom-lamp, wisdom-gem, non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view—this is called non-delusion.
These are the three wholesome roots.
^1-1063

Among these, which are the three unwholesome roots? Greed, hatred, delusion.
^1-1064

Among these, what is greed?
That which is lust,
passion,
seducing,
compliance,
delighting in,
taking lustful delight in,
heart's passion,
wanting,
languishing,
gulping at, devouring,
cupidity,
voracity,
cleaving to,
a slough,
longing,
illusion,
genitrix,
progenitrix,
seamstress,
she who ensnares,
the flowing stream,
she who is diffused,
the thread,
diffusion,
she who urges,
the consort,
aiming at,
she who leads to rebirth,
the forest,
the jungle,
intimacy,
fondness,
affection,
connexion,
appetite for,
hoping for,
anticipation,
appetite for visual shapes, for sounds, for odours, for tastes, for the tangible,
for getting, for wealth, for children, for life,
mumbling, mumbling on and over, muttering, murmuring,
self-indulgence, self-indulging, intemperateness,
agitation,
longing for the agreeable,
incestuous passion,
lawless greed,
wish,
hungering for,
entreating,
envying,
imploring,
craving for sensual indulgence, for existence, for non-existence,
for [material] form, for immateriality, for annihilation,
for visible shapes, for sounds, for smells, for tastes, for the tangible, for mental states,
a flood,
a yoke,
a tie,
grasping,
obstruction,
hindrance,
covering,
bondage,
depravity,
latent bias,
obsession,
a creeper,
avarice,
root of ill,
source of ill,
production of ill,
Māra's trap,
Māra's fish-hook,
Māra's domain,
craving, the flux of craving, the fishing-net of craving, the leash of craving,
the ocean of craving,
covetousness,
greed as the root of evil—this is what is called greed.
^1-1065

What is hatred? Resentment arises thinking: "He harmed me," "He is harming me," "He will harm me." Or thinking: "He harmed my dear and beloved one," "He is harming my dear and beloved one," "He will harm my dear and beloved one." Or thinking: "He benefited my disliked and unloved one," "He is benefiting my disliked and unloved one," "He will benefit my disliked and unloved one." Or resentment arises without reason. This mental resentment, repugnance, opposition, anger, wrath, ill-will, mental corruption, displeasure of mind, fury, fierceness, lack of satisfaction of mind—this is called hatred.
^1-1066

What is delusion? Ignorance of suffering, its origin, its cessation, and the path to its cessation. Ignorance of the past, future, and both past and future. Ignorance of dependent arising in conditionally arisen phenomena. This kind of ignorance, non-seeing, non-comprehension, non-awakening, non-realization, non-grasping, non-penetration, non-reflection, non-reviewing, non-direct knowledge, dullness, folly, lack of clear comprehension, delusion, confusion, ignorance, flood of ignorance, bond of ignorance, latent disposition of ignorance, obsession of ignorance, root of unwholesome kamma—this is called delusion.
These are the three unwholesome roots.
^1-1067

Which are the three indeterminate roots? Non-greed, non-hatred, non-delusion, whether as results of wholesome states or as functional indeterminate states—these are the three indeterminate roots.
^1-1068

Which are the nine sense-sphere roots? The three wholesome roots, the three unwholesome roots, the three indeterminate roots—these are the nine sense-sphere roots.
^1-1069

Which are the six fine-material-sphere roots? The three wholesome roots, the three indeterminate roots—these are the six fine-material-sphere roots.
^1-1070

Which are the six immaterial-sphere roots? The three wholesome roots, the three indeterminate roots—these are the six immaterial-sphere roots.
^1-1071

Which are the six unincluded roots? The three wholesome roots, the three indeterminate roots—these are the six unincluded roots.
^1-1072

Which are the three wholesome roots? Non-greed, non-hatred, non-delusion.
^1-1073

What is non-greed? That non-greed, not being greedy, the state of not being greedy, non-attachment, not being attached, the state of not being attached, non-covetousness, non-greed, the root of wholesome kamma—this is called non-greed.
^1-1074

Therein, what is non-hatred? That which is non-hatred, non-aversion, non-malice, non-ill-will, non-harming, is the wholesome root of non-hatred. This is called non-hatred.
^1-1075

Therein, what is non-delusion? Knowledge of suffering, knowledge of the origin of suffering, knowledge of the cessation of suffering, knowledge of the path leading to the cessation of suffering, knowledge of the past, knowledge of the future, knowledge of both past and future, knowledge of dependent arising in conditionally arisen phenomena; such wisdom, discernment, investigation, thorough investigation, investigation of phenomena, observation, close observation, repeated observation, sagacity, skill, dexterity, analysis, thought, examination, wisdom, intelligence, guidance, insight, clear comprehension, goad, wisdom, wisdom faculty, power of wisdom, weapon of wisdom, palace of wisdom, light of wisdom, aura of wisdom, radiance of wisdom, jewel of wisdom, non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view, the enlightenment factor of investigation of phenomena, a path factor, included in the path – this is called non-delusion.
These are the three wholesome roots.
^1-1076

Therein, what are the three indeterminate roots? Non-greed, non-hatred, non-delusion, as results of wholesome phenomena – these are the three indeterminate roots. These six are the unclassified roots – these phenomena are roots.
^1-1077

Which phenomena are not roots? Excluding those phenomena, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena of the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and not included in any sphere; the feeling aggregate, the perception aggregate, the formation aggregate, the consciousness aggregate; all matter, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are not roots.
^1-1078

Which phenomena are root-conditioned?
The feeling aggregate, the perception aggregate, the formation aggregate, the consciousness aggregate, which are root-conditioned by those phenomena (the roots) – these phenomena are root-conditioned.
^1-1079

Which phenomena are rootless? The feeling aggregate, the perception aggregate, the formation aggregate, the consciousness aggregate, which are rootless by those phenomena (the roots); all matter, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are rootless.
^1-1080

Which phenomena are associated with roots?
The feeling aggregate, the perception aggregate, the formation aggregate, the consciousness aggregate, which are associated with those phenomena (the roots) – these phenomena are associated with roots.
^1-1081

Which phenomena are dissociated from roots?
The feeling aggregate, the perception aggregate, the formation aggregate, the consciousness aggregate, which are dissociated from those phenomena (the roots); all matter, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are dissociated from roots.
^1-1082

Which phenomena are both roots and root-conditioned?
Greed with delusion is both a root and root-conditioned. Delusion with greed is both a root and root-conditioned. Hatred with delusion is both a root and root-conditioned. Delusion with hatred is both a root and root-conditioned.
Non-greed, non-hatred, non-delusion, they are mutually both roots and root-conditioned – these phenomena are both roots and root-conditioned.
^1-1083

Which phenomena are rooted but not roots?
The aggregates of feeling, perception, mental formations, and consciousness, excluding the roots themselves, are rooted but not roots.
^1-1084

Which phenomena are roots and associated with roots?
Greed with delusion, delusion with greed, hatred with delusion, delusion with hatred; non-greed, non-hatred, non-delusion are mutually roots and associated with roots.
^1-1085

Which phenomena are associated with roots but not roots?
The aggregates of feeling, perception, mental formations, and consciousness, excluding the roots themselves, are associated with roots but not roots.
^1-1086

Which phenomena are not roots but are rooted?
The aggregates of feeling, perception, mental formations, and consciousness are not roots but are rooted.
^1-1087

Which phenomena are not roots and are rootless?
The aggregates of feeling, perception, mental formations, and consciousness, all matter, and the unconditioned element are not roots and are rootless.
^1-1088

##### Minor Intermediate Pair ^1-3-2-2-0

Which phenomena are conditioned?
The five aggregates: the aggregate of form, feeling, perception, mental formations, and consciousness are conditioned.
^1-1089

Which phenomena are unconditioned?
The unconditioned element is unconditioned.
^1-1090

Which phenomena are conditioned?
Those phenomena that are conditioned (sappaccayā) are also conditioned (saṅkhatā).
^1-1091

Which phenomena are unconditioned?
That phenomenon which is unconditioned (appaccayā) is also unconditioned (asaṅkhatā).
^1-1092

What are visible phenomena?
Visible objects. These are visible phenomena.
^1-1093

What are invisible phenomena?
The eye-base... up to the tangible-object-base, the feeling aggregate... up to the consciousness aggregate, and matter that is invisible, non-impinging, included in the Dhamma-base, and the unconditioned element. These are invisible phenomena.
^1-1094

What are impinging phenomena?
The eye-base... up to the tangible-object-base. These are impinging phenomena.
^1-1095

What are non-impinging phenomena?
The feeling aggregate... up to the consciousness aggregate, and matter that is invisible, non-impinging, included in the Dhamma-base, and the unconditioned element. These are non-impinging phenomena.
^1-1096

What are material phenomena?
The four great primary elements and derived matter dependent on the four great primary elements. These are material phenomena.
^1-1097

What are immaterial phenomena?
The feeling aggregate... up to the consciousness aggregate, and the unconditioned element. These are immaterial phenomena.
^1-1098

What are mundane phenomena?
Tainted wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena belonging to the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and the matter aggregate... up to the consciousness aggregate. These are mundane phenomena.
^1-1099

What are supramundane phenomena?
The unincluded paths, and the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element. These are supramundane phenomena.
^1-1100

What are phenomena knowable by some (senses), not knowable by others?
Those phenomena knowable by eye-consciousness are not knowable by ear-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by ear-consciousness are not knowable by eye-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by eye-consciousness are not knowable by nose-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by nose-consciousness are not knowable by eye-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by eye-consciousness are not knowable by tongue-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by tongue-consciousness are not knowable by eye-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by eye-consciousness are not knowable by body-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by body-consciousness are not knowable by eye-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by ear-consciousness are not knowable by nose-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by nose-consciousness are not knowable by ear-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by ear-consciousness are not knowable by tongue-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by tongue-consciousness are not knowable by ear-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by ear-consciousness are not knowable by body-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by body-consciousness are not knowable by ear-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by ear-consciousness are not knowable by eye-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by eye-consciousness are not knowable by ear-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by nose-consciousness are not knowable by tongue-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by tongue-consciousness are not knowable by nose-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by nose-consciousness are not knowable by body-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by body-consciousness are not knowable by nose-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by nose-consciousness are not knowable by eye-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by eye-consciousness are not knowable by nose-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by nose-consciousness are not knowable by ear-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by ear-consciousness are not knowable by nose-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by tongue-consciousness are not knowable by body-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by body-consciousness are not knowable by tongue-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by tongue-consciousness are not knowable by eye-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by eye-consciousness are not knowable by tongue-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by tongue-consciousness are not knowable by ear-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by ear-consciousness are not knowable by tongue-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by tongue-consciousness are not knowable by nose-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by nose-consciousness are not knowable by tongue-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by body-consciousness are not knowable by eye-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by eye-consciousness are not knowable by body-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by body-consciousness are not knowable by ear-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by ear-consciousness are not knowable by body-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by body-consciousness are not knowable by nose-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by nose-consciousness are not knowable by body-consciousness.
Those phenomena knowable by body-consciousness are not knowable by tongue-consciousness; conversely, those phenomena knowable by tongue-consciousness are not knowable by body-consciousness. These are phenomena knowable by some (senses), not knowable by others.
^1-1101

##### The Taint Group ^1-3-2-3-0

Which are the states that are cankers? Four cankers – the canker of sensual desire, the canker of existence, the canker of views, and the canker of ignorance.
^1-1102

Therein, what is the canker of sensual desire? That desire, lust, delight, craving, affection, torment, infatuation, and clinging for sensual objects – this is called the canker of sensual desire.
^1-1103

Therein, what is the canker of existence? That desire, lust, delight, craving, affection, torment, infatuation, and clinging for existence – this is called the canker of existence.
^1-1104

Therein, what is the canker of views? Whether the world is eternal, or not eternal, or finite, or infinite, or the soul is the same as the body, or different from the body, or a Tathagata exists after death, or does not exist, or both exists and does not exist, or neither exists nor does not exist after death; whatever such view, wrong view, thicket of views, wilderness of views, perversion of views, agitation of views, fetter of views, grasping, adherence, dogmatic belief, perverted grasping, wrong path, false way, wrongness, heretical ground, perverted grasping – this is called the canker of views. All wrong views are the canker of views.
^1-1105

Therein, what is the canker of ignorance? Ignorance of suffering, its origin, its cessation, and the path to its cessation; ignorance of the past, of the future, of both, and of dependent arising in conditionally arisen phenomena; whatever such ignorance, non-seeing, non-comprehension, non-understanding, non-awakening, non-penetration, non-grasping, non-fathoming, non-reflection, non-reviewing, non-direct knowledge, dullness, folly, non-discernment, delusion, utter delusion, confusion, ignorance, flood of ignorance, bond of ignorance, latent disposition of ignorance, obsession of ignorance, chain of ignorance, delusion, root of unwholesome – this is called the canker of ignorance.
These states are cankers.
^1-1106

Which are the states that are not cankers?
Excluding those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate states belonging to the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and supramundane; the four mental aggregates; all matter, and the unconditioned element – these states are not cankers.
^1-1107

Which are the states that are associated with cankers?
Wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate states belonging to the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and immaterial sphere; the five aggregates – these states are associated with cankers.
^1-1108

Which are the states that are not associated with cankers?
The supramundane paths and their fruits, and the unconditioned element – these states are not associated with cankers.
^1-1109

Which are the states that are concomitant with cankers?
Those states that are concomitant with them – the four mental aggregates – these states are concomitant with cankers.
^1-1110

Which phenomena are disjoined from influxes?
Those phenomena that are disjoined from them (the influxes), namely, the feeling aggregate... up to... the consciousness aggregate; all matter; and the unconditioned element. These phenomena are disjoined from influxes.
^1-1111

Which phenomena are influxes and are tainted by influxes?
Those very influxes are influxes and are tainted by influxes.
^1-1112

Which phenomena are tainted by influxes but are not influxes?
Excluding those phenomena that are influxes, the remaining tainted wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and immaterial sphere; the matter aggregate... up to... the consciousness aggregate. These phenomena are tainted by influxes but are not influxes.
^1-1113

Which phenomena are influxes and are associated with influxes?
The sensual desire influx, when associated with the ignorance influx, is an influx and associated with an influx; the ignorance influx, when associated with the sensual desire influx, is an influx and associated with an influx; the desire for existence influx, when associated with the ignorance influx, is an influx and associated with an influx; the ignorance influx, when associated with the desire for existence influx, is an influx and associated with an influx; the wrong view influx, when associated with the ignorance influx, is an influx and associated with an influx; the ignorance influx, when associated with the wrong view influx, is an influx and associated with an influx. These phenomena are influxes and are associated with influxes.
^1-1114

Which phenomena are associated with influxes but are not influxes?
Excluding those phenomena that are influxes, the feeling aggregate... up to... the consciousness aggregate, when associated with influxes. These phenomena are associated with influxes but are not influxes.
^1-1115

Which phenomena are disjoined from influxes but are tainted by influxes?
Those phenomena that are disjoined from influxes but are tainted by influxes, namely, wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and immaterial sphere; the matter aggregate... up to... the consciousness aggregate. These phenomena are disjoined from influxes but are tainted by influxes.
^1-1116

Which phenomena are disjoined from influxes and are untainted by influxes?
The unincluded paths, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element. These phenomena are disjoined from influxes and are untainted by influxes.
The first recitation section in the Summary Chapter.
^1-1117

##### Group of Fetters ^1-3-2-4-0

Which phenomena are fetters?
Ten fetters: the fetter of sensual desire, the fetter of aversion, the fetter of conceit, the fetter of wrong view, the fetter of doubt, the fetter of clinging to rites and rituals, the fetter of desire for existence, the fetter of envy, the fetter of avarice, the fetter of ignorance.
^1-1118

What is the Fetter of sensual lust?
The desire, lust, delight, craving, affection, burning, infatuation, and clinging for sense objects. This is called the Fetter of sensual lust.
^1-1119

What is the Fetter of aversion?
Resentment arises thinking: 'He harmed me,' 'He is harming me,' 'He will harm me'; or similar thoughts regarding dear ones or disliked ones; or resentment arises without reason. Such mental resentment, opposition, aversion, hostility, anger, hatred, mental affliction, wrath, malice, enmity, fierceness, mental displeasure. This is called the Fetter of aversion.
^1-1120

What is the Fetter of conceit?
Conceit that 'I am better,' 'I am equal,' 'I am worse.' Such conceit, thinking, mental elevation, banner-like assertion, desire for prominence of mind. This is called the Fetter of conceit.
^1-1121

What is the Fetter of wrong view?
The view that the world is eternal or not, finite or infinite; that the soul is the same as the body or different; that a Tathāgata exists after death or not, or both, or neither. Such view, wrong view, thicket of views, perversion of views, agitation of views, fetter of views, grasping, clinging, adherence, wrong path, false way, heretical ground, grasping of perversion. This is called the Fetter of wrong view. Excluding the Fetter of adherence to rites and rituals, all wrong views are the Fetter of wrong view.
^1-1122

What is the Fetter of skeptical doubt?
Doubt and perplexity regarding the Teacher, Dhamma, Saṅgha, training, past, future, both past and future, and dependent arising. Such doubt, wavering, perplexity, indecision, uncertainty, hesitation, lack of penetration, mental stiffness, mental defilement. This is called the Fetter of skeptical doubt.
^1-1123

What is the Fetter of adherence to rites and rituals?
The view of recluses and brahmins outside this teaching that purification is by virtue, by ritual, or by virtue and ritual. Such view, wrong view, thicket of views, perversion of views, agitation of views, fetter of views, grasping, clinging, adherence, wrong path, false way, heretical ground, grasping of perversion. This is called the Fetter of adherence to rites and rituals.
^1-1124

What is the Fetter of lust for existence?
The desire, lust, delight, craving, affection, burning, infatuation, and clinging for existences. This is called the Fetter of lust for existence.
^1-1125

What is the Fetter of envy?
Envy, being envious, and jealousy regarding others' gain, honor, respect, veneration, and worship. This is called the Fetter of envy.
^1-1126

What is the Fetter of avarice?
The five avarices: regarding dwelling, family, gain, praise/beauty, Dhamma. Such miserliness, grudging, stinginess, harshness, mental ungenerosity. This is called the Fetter of avarice.
^1-1127

What is the Fetter of ignorance?
Ignorance of suffering, its origin, its cessation, the path to its cessation; ignorance of the past, future, both past and future; ignorance of dependent arising. Such ignorance, not seeing, non-comprehension, non-understanding, non-penetration, dullness, foolishness, lack of clear comprehension, delusion, extreme delusion, utter delusion, ignorance, flood of ignorance, bond of ignorance, latent disposition of ignorance, obsession of ignorance, chain of ignorance, delusion, root of unwholesome. This is called the Fetter of ignorance.
These phenomena are fetters.
^1-1128

Which phenomena are not fetters? Excluding those fetters, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena in the sense, fine-material, and immaterial spheres, and transcendent phenomena; the aggregates of feeling, perception, volitional formations, and consciousness; all matter, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are not fetters.
^1-1129

Which phenomena are fetter-producing? Tainted wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena in the sense, fine-material, and immaterial spheres; the five aggregates of form, feeling, perception, volitional formations, and consciousness – these phenomena are fetter-producing.
^1-1130

Which phenomena are not fetter-producing? Transcendent paths, their fruits, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are not fetter-producing.
^1-1131

Which phenomena are associated with fetters? Those phenomena which are associated with these fetters: the aggregates of feeling, perception, volitional formations, and consciousness – these phenomena are associated with fetters.
^1-1132

Which phenomena are dissociated from fetters? Those phenomena which are dissociated from these fetters: the aggregates of feeling, perception, volitional formations, and consciousness; all matter, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are dissociated from fetters.
^1-1133

Which phenomena are both fetters and fetter-producing? The fetters themselves are both fetters and fetter-producing.
^1-1134

Which phenomena are fetter-producing but not fetters? Excluding those fetters, the remaining tainted wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena in the sense, fine-material, and immaterial spheres; the five aggregates of form, feeling, perception, volitional formations, and consciousness – these phenomena are fetter-producing but not fetters.
^1-1135

Which phenomena are both fetters and associated with fetters? The sensuous lust fetter in conjunction with the ignorance fetter, and conversely, is both a fetter and associated with fetters. So is any one of the aversion, conceit, wrong view, doubt, adherence to rules and rituals, lust for existence, envy, and avarice fetters when in conjunction with the ignorance fetter, and conversely – these phenomena are both fetters and associated with fetters.
^1-1136

Which phenomena are associated with fetters but not fetters? Excluding those fetters, the aggregates of feeling, perception, volitional formations, and consciousness which are associated with these fetters – these phenomena are associated with fetters but not fetters.
^1-1137

Which states are disjoined from fetters but liable to fetters? Those states disjoined from these (fetters) that are tainted, wholesome, unwholesome, indeterminate states belonging to the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere; the aggregate of form... up to... the aggregate of consciousness
—these states are disjoined from fetters but liable to fetters.
^1-1138

Which states are disjoined from fetters and not liable to fetters? The unincluded paths, and the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element
—these states are disjoined from fetters and not liable to fetters.
^1-1139

##### The Bundle of Bodily Ties ^1-3-2-5-0

Which states are bodily ties? There are four bodily ties: the bodily tie of covetousness, the bodily tie of ill will, the bodily tie of adherence to rites and rituals, the bodily tie of dogmatic adherence to "this is the truth".
^1-1140

Among these, what is the bodily tie of covetousness? That which is lust, attachment, desire, craving, clinging, covetousness, greed, the root of unwholesomeness, a flood, a bond, a tie, a hindrance, a latent disposition, the root of suffering, Mara's snare
—this is called the bodily tie of covetousness.
^1-1141

Among these, what is the bodily tie of ill will? Resentment arises, thinking: "He harmed me," "He is harming me," "He will harm me." Resentment arises, thinking: "He harmed my dear, beloved one," "He is harming my dear, beloved one," "He will harm my dear, beloved one." Resentment arises, thinking: "He benefited my disliked, unloved one," "He is benefiting my disliked, unloved one," "He will benefit my disliked, unloved one." Or resentment arises without a reason. That such resentment, repulsion, hatred, ill will, anger, displeasure of mind
—this is called the bodily tie of ill will.
^1-1142

Among these, what is the bodily tie of adherence to rites and rituals?
That view of ascetics and brahmins outside (this teaching) that "purification is by virtue, purification is by ritual, purification is by virtue and ritual." That such a view, wrong view, fetter, grasping, dogmatic belief, adherence, wrong path, false path
—this is called the bodily tie of adherence to rites and rituals.
^1-1143

Among these, what is the bodily tie of dogmatic adherence to "this is the truth"?
The view that "The world is eternal, this alone is true, all else is false"; or "The world is not eternal..."; "The world is finite..."; "The world is infinite..."; "The soul is the body..."; "The soul is one thing, the body another..."; "The Tathagata exists after death..."; "The Tathagata does not exist after death..."; "The Tathagata both exists and does not exist after death..."; "The Tathagata neither exists nor does not exist after death, this alone is true, all else is false." That such a view, wrong view, fetter, grasping, dogmatic belief, adherence, wrong path, false path
—this is called the bodily tie of dogmatic adherence to "this is the truth."
Excluding the bodily tie of adherence to rites and rituals, all other wrong views are the bodily tie of dogmatic adherence to "this is the truth."
These states are bodily ties.
^1-1144

Which states are not bodily ties? Excluding these states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome, indeterminate states belonging to the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and the unincluded; the aggregate of feeling... up to... the aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element
—these states are not bodily ties.
^1-1145

Which phenomena are bound by fetters? Subject to taints, wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena of the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and immaterial sphere; the form aggregate... to the consciousness aggregate – these phenomena are bound by fetters.
^1-1146

Which phenomena are not bound by fetters? The unincluded paths, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are not bound by fetters.
^1-1147

Which phenomena are associated with fetters? Those phenomena that are associated with these fetters: the feeling aggregate... to the consciousness aggregate – these phenomena are associated with fetters.
^1-1148

Which phenomena are disassociated from fetters? Those phenomena that are disassociated from these fetters: the feeling aggregate... to the consciousness aggregate, all matter, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are disassociated from fetters.
^1-1149

Which phenomena are both fetters and bound by fetters? Those very fetters are both fetters and bound by fetters.
^1-1150

Which phenomena are bound by fetters but are not fetters? Excluding those phenomena that are fetters, the remaining subject to taints, wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena of the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and immaterial sphere; the form aggregate... to the consciousness aggregate – these phenomena are bound by fetters but are not fetters.
^1-1151

Which phenomena are both fetters and associated with fetters? The bodily fetter of adherence to rites and rituals with the bodily fetter of covetousness is both a fetter and associated with fetters; and the bodily fetter of covetousness with the bodily fetter of adherence to rites and rituals is both a fetter and associated with fetters; the bodily fetter of dogmatic belief with the bodily fetter of covetousness is both a fetter and associated with fetters; and the bodily fetter of covetousness with the bodily fetter of dogmatic belief is both a fetter and associated with fetters – these phenomena are both fetters and associated with fetters.
^1-1152

Which phenomena are associated with fetters but are not fetters? Excluding those phenomena that are associated with these fetters, the feeling aggregate... to the consciousness aggregate – these phenomena are associated with fetters but are not fetters.
^1-1153

Which phenomena are disassociated from fetters but bound by fetters? Those phenomena that are disassociated from these fetters: subject to taints, wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena of the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and immaterial sphere; the form aggregate... to the consciousness aggregate – these phenomena are disassociated from fetters but bound by fetters.
^1-1154

Which phenomena are disassociated from fetters and not bound by fetters? The unincluded paths, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are disassociated from fetters and not bound by fetters.
^1-1155

##### Group of Floods ^1-3-2-6-0

What are the states that are floods? The four floods…etc.… These states are dissociated from floods, conducive to floods.
^1-1156

##### Group of Yokes ^1-3-2-7-0

What are the states that are yokes? The four yokes…etc.… These states are dissociated from yokes, conducive to yokes.
^1-1157

##### Group of Hindrances ^1-3-2-8-0

What are the states that are hindrances? Six hindrances: the hindrance of sensual desire, the hindrance of ill will, the hindrance of sloth and torpor, the hindrance of restlessness and worry, the hindrance of doubt, the hindrance of ignorance.
^1-1158

Therein, what is the hindrance of sensual desire? Whatever sensual desire for sense objects, sensual lust, sensual delight, sensual craving, sensual affection, sensual burning, sensual infatuation, sensual clinging – this is called the hindrance of sensual desire.
^1-1159

Therein, what is the hindrance of ill will? "He acted to my detriment" – ill will arises; "He is acting to my detriment" – ill will arises; "He will act to my detriment" – ill will arises. Similarly, ill will arises concerning the detriment of a loved and desirable one, or the benefit of an unloved and undesirable one. Or ill will arises groundlessly. Whatever such ill will of mind, resentment, aversion, opposition, anger, intense anger, complete anger, hatred, malice, complete malice, mental affliction, mental defilement, wrath, being angry, state of being angry, hostility, state of being hostile, malevolence, state of being malevolent, enmity, fierceness, ill-temper, displeasure of mind – this is called the hindrance of ill will.
^1-1160

Therein, what is the hindrance of sloth and torpor? There is sloth, there is torpor.
^1-1161

Therein, what is sloth? Whatever mental unreadiness, unwieldiness, sinking, drooping, sluggishness, languor, state of being languid, sloth, state of being slothful – this is called sloth.
^1-1162

Among these, what is sloth? That unsuitability of the body, unworkability, being weighed down, enveloped, internally obstructed, sleepiness, drowsiness, the act of sleeping, the state of having slept – this is called sloth. Thus, this torpor and this sloth – this is called the hindrance of sloth and torpor.
^1-1163

Among these, what is the hindrance of restlessness and worry? There is restlessness, there is worry.
^1-1164

Among these, what is restlessness? That restlessness of mind, uncalmness, mental distraction, agitated state of mind – this is called restlessness.
^1-1165

Among these, what is worry? Perceiving as allowable what is not allowable, perceiving as not allowable what is allowable, perceiving as blameworthy what is not blameworthy, perceiving as not blameworthy what is blameworthy. Such worry, worrying, the state of having worried, mental regret, mental defilement – this is called worry. Thus, this restlessness and this worry – this is called the hindrance of restlessness and worry.
^1-1166

Among these, what is the hindrance of skeptical doubt? Doubts and is uncertain about the Teacher, the Dhamma, the Sangha, the training, the past, the future, both past and future, and dependent arising and its conditionally arisen phenomena. Such doubt, doubting, the state of having doubted, perplexity, uncertainty, being of two minds, vacillation, indecision, inability to penetrate, mental rigidity, mental defilement – this is called the hindrance of skeptical doubt.
^1-1167

Among these, what is the hindrance of ignorance? Ignorance of suffering, of the origin of suffering, of the cessation of suffering, of the path leading to the cessation of suffering, of the past, of the future, of both past and future, and of dependent arising and its conditionally arisen phenomena. Such ignorance, not seeing, non-comprehension, non-understanding, non-awakening, non-penetration, non-grasping, non-pervading, non-reflection, non-reviewing, non-realization, dullness, foolishness, lack of clear comprehension, delusion, utter delusion, confusion, ignorance, flood of ignorance, bond of ignorance, latent disposition of ignorance, obsession of ignorance, chain of ignorance, delusion, root of unwholesome states – this is called the hindrance of ignorance.
These phenomena are hindrances.
^1-1168

Which phenomena are not hindrances?
Excluding those phenomena, all remaining wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena in the sense-sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and the unincluded (supramundane); the four mental aggregates; all matter, and the unconditioned element (Nibbana) – these phenomena are not hindrances.
^1-1169

Which phenomena are hindrancable?
Contaminated wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena in the sense-sphere, fine-material sphere, and immaterial sphere; the five aggregates – these phenomena are hindrancable.
^1-1170

Which phenomena are not hindrancable?
The unincluded (supramundane) paths, and the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element (Nibbana) – these phenomena are not hindrancable.
^1-1171

What phenomena are associated with hindrances? The feeling aggregate, ... (and so on) ... the consciousness aggregate, which are associated with those [hindrances] – these phenomena are associated with hindrances.
^1-1172

What phenomena are dissociated from hindrances? The feeling aggregate, ... (and so on) ... the consciousness aggregate, which are dissociated from them; and all matter, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are dissociated from hindrances.
^1-1173

What phenomena are hindrances and also liable to be hindered? The hindrances themselves are hindrances and also liable to be hindered.
^1-1174

What phenomena are liable to be hindered but are not hindrances? Excluding those phenomena which are hindrances, the remaining taints-subject, wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and immaterial sphere; the matter aggregate, ... (and so on) ... the consciousness aggregate – these phenomena are liable to be hindered but are not hindrances.
^1-1175

What phenomena are hindrances and also associated with hindrances? The sensual desire hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the ignorance hindrance, and the ignorance hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the sensual desire hindrance; the ill will hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the ignorance hindrance, and the ignorance hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the ill will hindrance; the sloth-and-torpor hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the ignorance hindrance, and the ignorance hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the sloth-and-torpor hindrance; the restlessness hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the ignorance hindrance, and the ignorance hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the restlessness hindrance; the worry hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the ignorance hindrance, and the ignorance hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the worry hindrance; the doubt hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the ignorance hindrance, and the ignorance hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the doubt hindrance; the sensual desire hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the restlessness hindrance, and the restlessness hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the sensual desire hindrance; the ill will hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the restlessness hindrance, and the restlessness hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the ill will hindrance; the sloth-and-torpor hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the restlessness hindrance, and the restlessness hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the sloth-and-torpor hindrance; the worry hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the restlessness hindrance, and the restlessness hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the worry hindrance; the doubt hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the restlessness hindrance, and the restlessness hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the doubt hindrance; the ignorance hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the restlessness hindrance, and the restlessness hindrance is a hindrance and associated with the ignorance hindrance – these phenomena are hindrances and also associated with hindrances.
^1-1176

What phenomena are associated with hindrances but are not hindrances? Excluding those phenomena which are hindrances, the feeling aggregate, ... (and so on) ... the consciousness aggregate, which are associated with them – these phenomena are associated with hindrances but are not hindrances.
^1-1177

What phenomena are dissociated from hindrances but are liable to be hindered? The taints-subject, wholesome, and indeterminate phenomena in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and immaterial sphere, which are dissociated from them; the matter aggregate, ... (and so on) ... the consciousness aggregate – these phenomena are dissociated from hindrances but are liable to be hindered.
^1-1178

What phenomena are dissociated from hindrances and are not liable to be hindered? The unincluded paths, the path fruits, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are dissociated from hindrances and are not liable to be hindered.
^1-1179

##### Cluster on Misapprehension ^1-3-2-9-0

What phenomena are misapprehensions? Misapprehension of views.
^1-1180

What is clinging to views? 
 'The world is eternal' or 'the world is not eternal' or 'the world is finite' or 'the world is infinite' or 'the soul is the same as the body' or 'the soul is different from the body' or 'the Tathagata exists after death' or 'the Tathagata does not exist after death' or 'the Tathagata both exists and does not exist after death' or 'the Tathagata neither exists nor does not exist after death'—whatever such view, wrong view, thicket of views, wilderness of views, perversion of views, agitation of views, fetter of views, grasping, holding fast, adherence, clinging, wrong path, false way, wrongness, heretical ground, perverted grasping—this is called clinging to views. All wrong views are clinging to views.
These phenomena are clingings.
^1-1181

Which phenomena are not clingings? 
 Excluding those phenomena, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena belonging to the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and unincluded; the aggregate of feeling... up to... the aggregate of consciousness; and all matter, and the unconditioned element—these phenomena are not clingings.
^1-1182

Which phenomena are clung to? 
 Defilement-associated wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena belonging to the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere; the aggregate of form... up to... the aggregate of consciousness—these phenomena are clung to.
^1-1183

Which phenomena are not clung to? 
 The unincluded paths, and their fruits, and the unconditioned element—these phenomena are not clung to.
^1-1184

Which phenomena are associated with clinging to views? 
 Those phenomena that are associated with them: the aggregate of feeling... up to... the aggregate of consciousness—these phenomena are associated with clinging to views.
^1-1185

Which phenomena are disassociated from clinging to views? 
 Those phenomena that are disassociated from them: the aggregate of feeling... up to... the aggregate of consciousness; and all matter, and the unconditioned element—these phenomena are disassociated from clinging to views.
^1-1186

Which phenomena are both clinging and clung to? 
 That very clinging is both clinging and clung to.
^1-1187

Which phenomena are clung to but not clinging? 
 Those phenomena that are clung to, excluding those (the clingings), the remaining defilement-associated wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena belonging to the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere; the aggregate of form... up to... the aggregate of consciousness—these phenomena are clung to but not clinging.
^1-1188

Which phenomena are disassociated from clinging but clung to? 
 Those phenomena that are disassociated from them: defilement-associated wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena belonging to the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere; the aggregate of form... up to... the aggregate of consciousness—these phenomena are disassociated from clinging but clung to.
^1-1189

Which phenomena are disjoined from clinging and not clung to?
The unincluded paths, their fruits, and the unconditioned element. These phenomena are disjoined from clinging and not clung to.
^1-1190

##### Great Pair ^1-3-2-10-0

Which phenomena are with object?
The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of formations, the aggregate of consciousness. These phenomena are with object.
^1-1191

Which phenomena are without object?
All matter, and the unconditioned element. These phenomena are without object.
^1-1192

Which phenomena are consciousness?
Eye-consciousness, ear-consciousness, nose-consciousness, tongue-consciousness, body-consciousness, mind-element, mind-consciousness element. These phenomena are consciousness.
^1-1193

Which phenomena are not consciousness?
The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of formations, all matter, and the unconditioned element. These phenomena are not consciousness.
^1-1194

Which phenomena are mental factors?
The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of formations. These phenomena are mental factors.
^1-1195

Which phenomena are not mental factors?
Consciousness, all matter, and the unconditioned element. These phenomena are not mental factors.
^1-1196

Which phenomena are associated with consciousness?
The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of formations. These phenomena are associated with consciousness.
^1-1197

Which phenomena are dissociated from consciousness?
All matter, and the unconditioned element. These phenomena are dissociated from consciousness. Consciousness should not be described as associated with consciousness, nor as dissociated from consciousness.
^1-1198

Which states are conjoined with mind?
The feeling aggregate, perception aggregate, and volitional formations aggregate. These states are conjoined with mind.
^1-1199

Which states are disjoined from mind?
All matter and the unconditioned element. These states are disjoined from mind. Mind should not be said to be conjoined with mind, nor disjoined from mind.
^1-1200

Which states arise from mind?
The feeling aggregate, perception aggregate, volitional formations aggregate; bodily intimation, vocal intimation; and whatever other mind-born, mind-rooted, mind-originated matter there is, such as the visible form base, sound base, smell base, taste base, tangible object base, space element, water element, lightness of matter, malleability of matter, wieldiness of matter, production of matter, continuity of matter, and edible food. These states arise from mind.
^1-1201

Which states do not arise from mind?
Mind, the remaining matter, and the unconditioned element. These states do not arise from mind.
^1-1202

Which states are co-existent with mind?
The feeling aggregate, perception aggregate, volitional formations aggregate, bodily intimation, and vocal intimation. These states are co-existent with mind.
^1-1203

Which states are not co-existent with mind?
Mind, the remaining matter, and the unconditioned element. These states are not co-existent with mind.
^1-1204

Which states follow the mind?
The feeling aggregate, perception aggregate, volitional formations aggregate, bodily intimation, and vocal intimation. These states follow the mind.
^1-1205

Which states do not follow the mind?
Mind, the remaining matter, and the unconditioned element. These states do not follow the mind.
^1-1206

Which states are conjoined with mind and arise from mind?
The feeling aggregate, perception aggregate, and volitional formations aggregate. These states are conjoined with mind and arise from mind.
^1-1207

Which states are not conjoined with mind and do not arise from mind?
Mind, all matter, and the unconditioned element. These states are not conjoined with mind and do not arise from mind.
^1-1208

Which states are conjoined with, arisen from, and co-existent with consciousness? The feeling aggregate, the perception aggregate, the volitional formations aggregate – these states are conjoined with, arisen from, and co-existent with consciousness.
^1-1209

Which states are not conjoined with, arisen from, and co-existent with consciousness? Consciousness, all matter, and the unconditioned element – these states are not conjoined with, arisen from, and co-existent with consciousness.
^1-1210

Which states follow the arising of consciousness and are conjoined with it? The feeling aggregate, the perception aggregate, the volitional formations aggregate – these states follow the arising of consciousness and are conjoined with it.
^1-1211

Which states do not follow the arising of consciousness and are conjoined with it? Consciousness, all matter, and the unconditioned element – these states do not follow the arising of consciousness and are conjoined with it.
^1-1212

Which states are 
internal?
The eye-base... up to... the mind-base – these states are internal.
^1-1213

Which states are 
external?
The visible form-base... up to... the phenomena-base – these states are external.
^1-1214

Which states are 
derived?
The eye-base... up to... edible food – these states are derived.
^1-1215

Which states are 
not derived?
The feeling aggregate, the perception aggregate, the volitional formations aggregate, the consciousness aggregate, the four great essential elements, and the unconditioned element – these states are not derived.
^1-1216

Which states are 
grasped by kamma?
The tainted results of wholesome and unwholesome states belonging to the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and immaterial sphere; the feeling aggregate... up to... the consciousness aggregate; and matter produced by kamma – these states are grasped by kamma.
^1-1217

Which states are 
not grasped by kamma?
The tainted wholesome and unwholesome states belonging to the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and immaterial sphere; the feeling aggregate... up to... the consciousness aggregate; and functional states that are neither wholesome nor unwholesome nor kamma-results; and matter not produced by kamma; and the unincluded paths and path-fruits; and the unconditioned element – these states are not grasped by kamma.
^1-1218

##### Section on Clinging ^1-3-2-11-0

Which phenomena are clingings? Four clingings: sensual clinging, clinging to views, clinging to rites and rituals, clinging to a doctrine of self.
^1-1219

What is sensual clinging? That desire for sensual pleasures, lust for sensual pleasures, delight in sensual pleasures, craving for sensual pleasures, affection for sensual pleasures, agitation due to sensual pleasures, infatuation with sensual pleasures, obsession with sensual pleasures – this is called sensual clinging.
^1-1220

What is clinging to views? The view that there is no giving, no offering, no sacrifice; no fruit or result of good and bad actions; no this world, no other world; no mother, no father; no spontaneously born beings; no recluses and brahmins in the world who are rightly gone, rightly practiced, who have themselves realized and proclaimed this world and the other world by direct knowledge – such a view, wrong view, thicket of views, wilderness of views, agitation of views, fickleness of views, fetter of views, grasp, holding, adherence, clinging, wrong path, false way, wrongness, heretical tenet, perverse grasp – this is called clinging to views. Excluding clinging to rites and rituals and clinging to a doctrine of self, all wrong views are clinging to views.
^1-1221

What is clinging to rites and rituals? Among recluses and brahmins outside [this dispensation], the view that purification is through virtue, purification is through vows, purification is through rites and rituals – such a view, wrong view, thicket of views, wilderness of views, agitation of views, fickleness of views, fetter of views, grasp, holding, adherence, clinging, wrong path, false way, wrongness, heretical tenet, perverse grasp – this is called clinging to rites and rituals.
^1-1222

What is clinging to a doctrine of self? Here, an uninstructed worldling, not seeing noble ones, not skilled in the noble Dhamma, not trained in the noble Dhamma; not seeing true men, not skilled in the Dhamma of true men, not trained in the Dhamma of true men – perceives form as self, or self as possessing form, or form as being in self, or self as being in form. Feeling... perception... mental formations... consciousness he perceives as self, or self as possessing consciousness, or consciousness as being in self, or self as being in consciousness. Such a view, wrong view, thicket of views, wilderness of views, agitation of views, fickleness of views, fetter of views, grasp, holding, adherence, clinging, wrong path, false way, wrongness, heretical tenet, perverse grasp – this is called clinging to a doctrine of self.
These phenomena are clingings.
^1-1223

Which phenomena are not clingings? Excluding those phenomena, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and unincluded; the aggregate of feeling... consciousness; and all matter, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are not clingings.
^1-1224

Which phenomena are objects of clinging? Wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena with taints in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and immaterial sphere; the aggregate of form... consciousness – these phenomena are objects of clinging.
^1-1225

Which phenomena are not objects of clinging? The unincluded paths and their fruits, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are not objects of clinging.
^1-1226

Which phenomena are associated with clinging? The mental aggregates (feeling, perception, mental formations, and consciousness) that are associated with clinging – these phenomena are associated with clinging.
^1-1227

Which phenomena are dissociated from clinging? The mental aggregates (feeling, perception, mental formations, and consciousness) that are dissociated from clinging; all physical form, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are dissociated from clinging.
^1-1228

Which phenomena are both clinging and objects of clinging? The clingings themselves are both clinging and objects of clinging.
^1-1229

Which phenomena are objects of clinging but not clinging itself? Excluding the clingings themselves, the remaining defiled wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena pertaining to the sense-sphere, form-sphere, and formless-sphere; the five aggregates (form, feeling, perception, mental formations, and consciousness) – these phenomena are objects of clinging but not clinging itself.
^1-1230

Which phenomena are both clinging and associated with clinging? Clinging to views associated with sensual clinging is both clinging and associated with clinging; sensual clinging associated with clinging to views is both clinging and associated with clinging; clinging to rites and rituals associated with sensual clinging is both clinging and associated with clinging; sensual clinging associated with clinging to rites and rituals is both clinging and associated with clinging; clinging to a doctrine of self associated with sensual clinging is both clinging and associated with clinging; sensual clinging associated with clinging to a doctrine of self is both clinging and associated with clinging – these phenomena are both clinging and associated with clinging.
^1-1231

Which phenomena are associated with clinging but not clinging itself? Excluding the clingings themselves, the mental aggregates (feeling, perception, mental formations, and consciousness) that are associated with clinging – these phenomena are associated with clinging but not clinging itself.
^1-1232

Which phenomena are dissociated from clinging yet are objects of clinging? Those phenomena that are dissociated from clinging, namely, defiled wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena pertaining to the sense-sphere, form-sphere, and formless-sphere; the five aggregates (form, feeling, perception, mental formations, and consciousness) – these phenomena are dissociated from clinging yet are objects of clinging.
^1-1233

Which phenomena are dissociated from clinging and are not objects of clinging? The supramundane paths, and their fruits, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are dissociated from clinging and are not objects of clinging.
The second recitation section in the Summary Chapter.
^1-1234

##### Cluster of Defilements ^1-3-2-12-0

What are defilements? The ten bases of defilements are: greed, hatred, delusion, conceit, wrong view, skeptical doubt, sloth, restlessness, shamelessness, and fearlessness of wrongdoing.
^1-1235

Therein, what is greed? That which is lust, attachment, desire, infatuation, clinging, covetousness; craving for sensuality, existence, non-existence, fine-material, immaterial, cessation, forms, sounds, smells, tastes, tangibles, mental objects; the flood, bond, knot, clinging, hindrance, latent disposition, root of suffering, covetousness, greed, the unwholesome root. This is called greed.
^1-1236

Therein, what is hatred? Ill-will arises concerning harm done to oneself or loved ones, or benefit to disliked ones, in the past, present, or future; or ill-will arises without reason. That which is such ill-will, aversion, opposition, anger, wrath, hatred, mental affliction, mental displeasure, fury, animosity, mental non-satisfaction. This is called hatred.
^1-1237

Therein, what is delusion? Ignorance of suffering, its origin, its cessation, and the path to its cessation; ignorance of the past, future, both past and future; ignorance of dependent arising and dependently arisen phenomena. That which is such ignorance, non-seeing, non-comprehension, dullness, foolishness, delusion, utter delusion, confusion, ignorance, flood of ignorance, bond of ignorance, latent disposition of ignorance, defilement of ignorance, delusion, the unwholesome root. This is called delusion.
^1-1238

Therein, what is conceit? Conceit thinking: 'I am superior,' 'I am equal,' 'I am inferior.' That which is such conceit, conceiving, elevation, upliftment, a banner, excessive grasping, desire for prominence of mind. This is called conceit.
^1-1239

Therein, what is wrong view? The view: 'The world is eternal,' or 'The world is not eternal,' or 'The world is finite,' or 'The world is infinite'; 'The soul is the same as the body,' or 'The soul is different from the body'; 'A Tathāgata exists after death,' or 'A Tathāgata does not exist after death,' or 'A Tathāgata both exists and does not exist after death,' or 'A Tathāgata neither exists nor does not exist after death.' That which is such view, wrong view, thicket of views, wilderness of views, contortion of views, vacillation of views, fetter of views, grasping, adherence, dogmatic belief, misapprehension, wrong path, wrongness, heretical ground, perverted grasping. This is called wrong view. All wrong views are (called) view.
^1-1240

Therein, what is skeptical doubt? Doubts and is uncertain about the Teacher, the Dhamma, the Saṅgha, the training; about the past, future, both past and future; about dependent arising and dependently arisen phenomena. That which is such doubt, wavering, uncertainty, perplexity, indecision, two-pronged path, uncertainty, hesitation, mental stiffness, mental defilement. This is called skeptical doubt.
^1-1241

Therein, what is sloth? That which is mental unsuitability, unworkability, sinking, drooping, sluggishness, languor, sloth of mind. This is called sloth.
^1-1242

Therein, what is restlessness? That which is mental agitation, non-calmness, distraction, bewilderment of mind. This is called restlessness.
^1-1243

Therein, what is shamelessness? That which is not being ashamed of what should be shameful, not being ashamed of committing evil, unwholesome actions. This is called shamelessness.
^1-1244

What is fearlessness of wrongdoing? Not dreading what should be dreaded, not dreading the committing of evil, unwholesome phenomena – this is called fearlessness of wrongdoing.
These phenomena are defilements.
^1-1245

Which phenomena are not defilements? Excluding those defilements, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena in the sense, fine-material, immaterial, and supramundane spheres; the feeling aggregate... up to the consciousness aggregate; all matter, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are not defilements.
^1-1246

Which phenomena are conducive to defilement? Wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena subject to taints in the sense, fine-material, and immaterial spheres; the matter aggregate... up to the consciousness aggregate – these phenomena are conducive to defilement.
^1-1247

Which phenomena are not conducive to defilement? Supramundane paths, path fruitions, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are not conducive to defilement.
^1-1248

Which phenomena are actually defiled? The three unwholesome roots – greed, hatred, delusion; and defilements co-existent with them, the feeling aggregate... up to the consciousness aggregate associated with them, and bodily, verbal, and mental kamma originating from them – these phenomena are actually defiled.
^1-1249

Which phenomena are undefiled? Wholesome and indeterminate phenomena in the sense, fine-material, immaterial, and supramundane spheres; the feeling aggregate... up to the consciousness aggregate; all matter, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are undefiled.
^1-1250

Which phenomena are associated with defilements? The feeling aggregate... up to the consciousness aggregate that are associated with them – these phenomena are associated with defilements.
^1-1251

Which phenomena are dissociated from defilements? The feeling aggregate... up to the consciousness aggregate that are dissociated from them; all matter, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are dissociated from defilements.
^1-1252

Which phenomena are both defilements and conducive to defilement? Those very defilements are both defilements and conducive to defilement.
^1-1253

Which phenomena are defiling but not defilements? Excluding those phenomena that are defiling, the remaining taints-associated wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and immaterial sphere; the aggregate of form... up to... the aggregate of consciousness – these phenomena are defiling but not defilements.
^1-1254

Which phenomena are defilements and are also defiled? The defilements themselves are defilements and are also defiled.
^1-1255

Which phenomena are defiled but not defilements? Excluding those phenomena that are defiled, the aggregate of feeling... up to... the aggregate of consciousness – these phenomena are defiled but not defilements.
^1-1256

Which phenomena are defilements and are associated with defilements? Greed, hatred, delusion, conceit, wrong view, doubt, sloth, restlessness, shamelessness, and fearlessness of wrongdoing, when associated with each other in various pairings, are defilements and are associated with defilements.
^1-1257

Which phenomena are associated with defilements but are not defilements? Excluding those phenomena that are associated, the aggregate of feeling... up to... the aggregate of consciousness – these phenomena are associated with defilements but are not defilements.
^1-1258

Which phenomena are dissociated from defilements but are defiling? Excluding those phenomena that are dissociated, the taints-associated wholesome and indeterminate phenomena in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and immaterial sphere; the aggregate of form... up to... the aggregate of consciousness – these phenomena are dissociated from defilements but are defiling.
^1-1259

Which phenomena are dissociated from defilements and are not defiling? The unincluded paths, and their fruits, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are dissociated from defilements and are not defiling.
^1-1260

##### ⤵The Section on Abandonment. ^1-3-2-13-0

Which phenomena are to be abandoned by vision? The three fetters: identity view, doubt, and clinging to rites and rituals.
^1-1261

What is identity view? Here, an uninstructed ordinary person, not seeing the noble ones, unskilled in the noble Dhamma, undisciplined in the noble Dhamma, not seeing the true persons, unskilled in the Dhamma of the true persons, undisciplined in the Dhamma of the true persons, perceives form as self, or self as possessing form, or form as in self, or self as in form. Feeling... perception... mental formations... consciousness as self, or self as possessing consciousness, or consciousness as in self, or self as in consciousness. Whatever such view, wrong view... up to... grasping of perversion – this is called identity view.
^1-1262

Among these, what is doubt? Wavering, doubting concerning the Teacher... stiffness of mind, mental defilement – this is called doubt.
^1-1263

Among these, what is clinging to rites and rituals? The view among recluses and brahmins outside [this dispensation] that purity comes from morality, purity comes from observances, purity comes from morality and observances – such a view, wrong view... mistaken apprehension – this is called clinging to rites and rituals. These three fetters, and defilements rooted in them, the feeling aggregate... consciousness aggregate associated with them, bodily action, vocal action, mental action originating from them – these states are to be abandoned by seeing.
^1-1264

Which states are not to be abandoned by seeing? Excluding those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate states in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and unincluded; the feeling aggregate... consciousness aggregate; all matter, and the unconditioned element – these states are not to be abandoned by seeing.
^1-1265

Which states are to be abandoned by development? The remaining greed, hatred, and delusion, and defilements rooted in them, the feeling aggregate... consciousness aggregate associated with them, bodily action, vocal action, mental action originating from them – these states are to be abandoned by development.
^1-1266

Which states are not to be abandoned by development? Excluding those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate states in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and unincluded; the feeling aggregate... consciousness aggregate; all matter, and the unconditioned element – these states are not to be abandoned by development.
^1-1267

Which states have causes to be abandoned by seeing? The three fetters: identity view, doubt, clinging to rites and rituals.
^1-1268

Among these, what is identity view? Here, an uninstructed ordinary person, not seeing noble ones, unskilled in the noble Dhamma, not trained in the noble Dhamma, not seeing true persons, unskilled in the Dhamma of true persons, not trained in the Dhamma of true persons, regards form as self, or self as possessing form, or form as in self, or self as in form. Similarly, regards feeling... perception... mental formations... consciousness as self, or self as possessing consciousness, or consciousness as in self, or self as in consciousness. Such a view, wrong view... mistaken apprehension – this is called identity view.
^1-1269

Among these, what is doubt? Wavering, doubting concerning the Teacher... stiffness of mind, mental defilement – this is called doubt.
^1-1270

Among these, what is clinging to rites and rituals? The view among recluses and brahmins outside [this dispensation] that purity comes from morality, purity comes from observances, purity comes from morality and observances – such a view, wrong view... mistaken apprehension – this is called clinging to rites and rituals. These three fetters, and defilements rooted in them, the feeling aggregate... consciousness aggregate associated with them, bodily action, vocal action, mental action originating from them – these states have causes to be abandoned by seeing.
