The three fetters – identity view, doubt, clinging to rites and rituals – these states are to be abandoned by seeing. Greed, hatred, and delusion rooted in them – these states are causes to be abandoned by seeing. And defilements rooted in them, the feeling aggregate... consciousness aggregate associated with them, bodily action, vocal action, mental action originating from them – these states have causes to be abandoned by seeing.
^1-1271

Which states do not have causes to be abandoned by seeing? Excluding those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate states in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and unincluded; the feeling aggregate... consciousness aggregate; all matter, and the unconditioned element – these states do not have causes to be abandoned by seeing.
^1-1272

Which phenomena have causes to be abandoned by development? The remaining greed, hatred, delusion – these phenomena are causes to be abandoned by development. And defilements associated with them, the feeling aggregate...pe...the consciousness aggregate associated with them, and bodily, verbal, and mental kamma originated from them – these phenomena have causes to be abandoned by development.
^1-1273

Which phenomena do not have causes to be abandoned by development? Excluding those phenomena, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and supramundane; the feeling aggregate...pe...the consciousness aggregate; and all material phenomena, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena do not have causes to be abandoned by development.
^1-1274

Which phenomena are accompanied by initial application? In the sphere of initial application, in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and supramundane, excluding initial application, the feeling aggregate...pe...the consciousness aggregate associated with it – these phenomena are accompanied by initial application.
^1-1275

Which phenomena are not accompanied by initial application? In the sphere without initial application, in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and supramundane; the feeling aggregate...pe...the consciousness aggregate; and initial application, and all material phenomena, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are not accompanied by initial application.
^1-1276

Which phenomena are accompanied by sustained application? In the sphere of sustained application, in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and supramundane, excluding sustained application, the feeling aggregate, the perception aggregate, the mental formations aggregate, the consciousness aggregate associated with it – these phenomena are accompanied by sustained application.
^1-1277

Which phenomena are not accompanied by sustained application? In the sphere without sustained application, in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and supramundane; the feeling aggregate...pe...the consciousness aggregate; and sustained application, and all material phenomena, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are not accompanied by sustained application.
^1-1278

Which phenomena are accompanied by rapture? In the sphere of rapture, in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and supramundane, excluding rapture, the feeling aggregate...pe...the consciousness aggregate associated with it – these phenomena are accompanied by rapture.
^1-1279

Which phenomena are not accompanied by rapture? In the sphere without rapture, in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and supramundane; the feeling aggregate...pe...the consciousness aggregate; and rapture, and all material phenomena, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are not accompanied by rapture.
^1-1280

Which phenomena are concomitant with rapture? In the sphere of rapture, in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, and supramundane, excluding rapture, the feeling aggregate...pe...the consciousness aggregate associated with it – these phenomena are concomitant with rapture.
^1-1281

Which phenomena are not concomitant with rapture? In the sphere without rapture, in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and supramundane; the feeling aggregate...pe...the consciousness aggregate; and rapture, and all material phenomena, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are not concomitant with rapture.
^1-1282

Which phenomena are accompanied by pleasure?
In the plane of pleasure, in the sense-sphere, form-sphere, and the unincluded, excluding pleasure itself, the associated aggregate of perception, aggregate of formations, and aggregate of consciousness – these phenomena are accompanied by pleasure.
^1-1283

Which phenomena are not accompanied by pleasure?
Not in the plane of pleasure, in the sense-sphere, form-sphere, formless-sphere, or unincluded; the aggregate of feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness; and pleasure itself, all matter, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are not accompanied by pleasure.
^1-1284

Which phenomena are accompanied by equanimity?
In the plane of equanimity, in the sense-sphere, form-sphere, formless-sphere, and the unincluded, excluding equanimity itself, the associated aggregate of perception, aggregate of formations, and aggregate of consciousness – these phenomena are accompanied by equanimity.
^1-1285

Which phenomena are not accompanied by equanimity?
Not in the plane of equanimity, in the sense-sphere, form-sphere, or unincluded; the aggregate of feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness; and equanimity itself, all matter, and the unconditioned element – these phenomena are not accompanied by equanimity.
^1-1286

Which phenomena belong to the sense-sphere?
Taking Avīci hell as the lower boundary, and the Paranimmita-vasavattī devas as the upper boundary, whatever aggregates, elements, and sense-bases are included within this range, namely matter, feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness – these phenomena belong to the sense-sphere.
^1-1287

Which phenomena do not belong to the sense-sphere?
Phenomena of the form-sphere, formless-sphere, and the unincluded – these phenomena do not belong to the sense-sphere.
^1-1288

Which phenomena belong to the form-sphere?
Taking the Brahma-world as the lower boundary, and the Akanittha devas as the upper boundary, whatever mental states and mental factors are included within this range, belonging to one who has attained, or one who has been reborn, or one who dwells in happiness in this very life – these phenomena belong to the form-sphere.
^1-1289

Which phenomena do not belong to the form-sphere?
Phenomena of the sense-sphere, formless-sphere, and the unincluded – these phenomena do not belong to the form-sphere.
^1-1290

Which phenomena belong to the formless-sphere?
Taking the devas of the base of infinite space as the lower boundary, and the devas of the base of neither perception nor non-perception as the upper boundary, whatever mental states and mental factors are included within this range, belonging to one who has attained, or one who has been reborn, or one who dwells in happiness in this very life – these phenomena belong to the formless-sphere.
^1-1291

Which phenomena do not belong to the formless-sphere?
Phenomena of the sense-sphere, form-sphere, and the unincluded – these phenomena do not belong to the formless-sphere.
^1-1292

Which phenomena are included?
Phenomena subject to taints, wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate, in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere; the form aggregate... up to... the consciousness aggregate—these phenomena are included.
^1-1293

Which phenomena are not included?
The paths, the path fruits, and the unconditioned element—these phenomena are not included.
^1-1294

Which phenomena lead to liberation?
The four not-included paths—these phenomena lead to liberation.
^1-1295

Which phenomena do not lead to liberation?
Apart from those phenomena, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and the not-included; the feeling aggregate... up to... the consciousness aggregate, all matter, and the unconditioned element—these phenomena do not lead to liberation.
^1-1296

Which phenomena are fixed?
The five heinous actions, fixed wrong view, and the four not-included paths—these phenomena are fixed.
^1-1297

Which phenomena are not fixed?
Apart from those phenomena, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and the not-included; the feeling aggregate... up to... the consciousness aggregate, all matter, and the unconditioned element—these phenomena are not fixed.
^1-1298

Which phenomena have a superior?
Phenomena subject to taints, wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate, in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere; the form aggregate... up to... the consciousness aggregate—these phenomena have a superior.
^1-1299

Which phenomena have no superior?
The not-included paths, the path fruits, and the unconditioned element—these phenomena have no superior.
^1-1300

Which phenomena are with danger?
The three unwholesome roots: greed, hatred, delusion; and defilements associated with them; the feeling aggregate... up to... the consciousness aggregate associated with them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action arising from them—these phenomena are with danger.
^1-1301

Which phenomena are without danger?
Wholesome and indeterminate phenomena in the sense sphere, fine-material sphere, immaterial sphere, and the not-included; the feeling aggregate... up to... the consciousness aggregate, all matter, and the unconditioned element—these phenomena are without danger.
Abhidhamma Dyads.
^1-1302

#### Exposition of Suttanta Dyads ^1-3-3-0

Which are the states partaking of knowledge? States associated with knowledge – these states partake of knowledge.
^1-1303

Which are the states partaking of ignorance? States associated with ignorance – these states partake of ignorance.
^1-1304

Which are the states like lightning? Wisdom in the three lower noble paths – these states are like lightning.
^1-1305

Which are the states like a diamond? Wisdom in the Arahant path – these states are like a diamond.
^1-1306

Which are the states that are foolish? Shamelessness and fearlessness of wrongdoing – these states are foolish. All unwholesome states are foolish.
^1-1307

Which are the states that are wise? Shame and fear of wrongdoing – these states are wise. All wholesome states are wise.
^1-1308

Which are the states that are dark? Shamelessness and fearlessness of wrongdoing – these states are dark. All unwholesome states are dark.
^1-1309

Which are the states that are bright? Shame and fear of wrongdoing – these states are bright. All wholesome states are bright.
^1-1310

What are the states that are to be tormented? Bodily misconduct, verbal misconduct, mental misconduct – these states are to be tormented. All unwholesome states are to be tormented.
^1-1311

What are the states that are not to be tormented? Bodily good conduct, verbal good conduct, mental good conduct – these states are not to be tormented. All wholesome states are not to be tormented.
^1-1312

What are the states that are designations? The reckoning, appellation, concept, usage, name, naming, nomenclature, expression, indication, and utterance of those various states – these states are designations. All states are paths of designation.
^1-1313

What are the states that are expressions? The reckoning, appellation, concept, usage, name, naming, nomenclature, expression, indication, and utterance of those various states – these states are expressions. All states are paths of expression.
^1-1314

What are the states that are concepts? The reckoning, appellation, concept, usage, name, naming, nomenclature, expression, indication, and utterance of those various states – these states are concepts. All states are paths of concepts.
^1-1315

Therein, what is name? The feeling aggregate, the perception aggregate, the volitional formations aggregate, the consciousness aggregate, and the unconditioned element – this is called name.
^1-1316

Therein, what is form? The four great primary elements, and form derived from the four great primary elements – this is called form.
^1-1317

Therein, what is ignorance? That which is not knowing, not seeing...pe... the fetter of ignorance, delusion, the root of unwholesome states – this is called ignorance.
^1-1318

Therein, what is craving for existence? That which is desire for existence in states of being...pe... clinging to existence – this is called craving for existence.
^1-1319

Therein, what is view of existence? The view 'there will be a self and a world,' such a view, a wrong view...pe... a perverted grasp – this is called view of existence.
^1-1320

What is the view of non-existence? The view, 'There will be no self and no world,' such a view, wrong view, perverted grasping – this is called the view of non-existence.
^1-1321

What is the view of eternalism? The view, 'The self and the world are eternal,' such a view, wrong view, perverted grasping – this is called the view of eternalism.
^1-1322

What is the view of annihilationism? The view, 'The self and the world will be annihilated,' such a view, wrong view, perverted grasping – this is called the view of annihilationism.
^1-1323

What is the view of finitude? The view, 'The self and the world are finite,' such a view, wrong view, perverted grasping – this is called the view of finitude.
^1-1324

What is the view of infinitude? The view, 'The self and the world are infinite,' such a view, wrong view, perverted grasping – this is called the view of infinitude.
^1-1325

What is speculation about the past? The view that arises concerning the past, wrong view, perverted grasping – this is called speculation about the past.
^1-1326

What is speculation about the future? The view that arises concerning the future, wrong view, perverted grasping – this is called speculation about the future.
^1-1327

What is shamelessness? That which is not ashamed of what should be ashamed of, is not ashamed of committing evil, unwholesome states – this is called shamelessness.
^1-1328

What is fearlessness of wrongdoing? That which is not afraid of what should be feared, is not afraid of committing evil, unwholesome states – this is called fearlessness of wrongdoing.
^1-1329

What is moral shame? That which is ashamed of what should be ashamed of, is ashamed of committing evil, unwholesome states – this is called moral shame.
^1-1330

What is fear of wrongdoing? The fear of what should be feared, the fear of committing evil unwholesome phenomena – this is called fear of wrongdoing.
^1-1331

What is unruliness? When spoken to by fellow Dhamma practitioners, unruliness, taking advice adversely, habitual antagonism, disrespect, lack of deference, lack of reverence, not heeding – this is called unruliness.
^1-1332

What is bad friendship? The association, frequent association, close companionship, devotion, or inclination towards those individuals who are faithless, immoral, unlearned, stingy, unwise – this is called bad friendship.
^1-1333

What is docility? When spoken to by fellow Dhamma practitioners, docility, not taking advice adversely, not habitually antagonistic, respect, deference, reverence, heeding – this is called docility.
^1-1334

What is good friendship? The association, frequent association, close companionship, devotion, or inclination towards those individuals who are faithful, virtuous, learned, generous, wise – this is called good friendship.
^1-1335

What is skill in offenses? The five categories of offenses, the seven categories of offenses. The wisdom, understanding, non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view concerning those offenses – this is called skill in offenses.
^1-1336

What is skill in rising from offenses? The wisdom, understanding, non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view concerning rising from those offenses – this is called skill in rising from offenses.
^1-1337

What is skill in attainments? There is attainment with initial and sustained application, there is attainment with only sustained application, there is attainment without initial and sustained application. The wisdom, understanding, non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view concerning those attainments – this is called skill in attainments.
^1-1338

What is skill in rising from attainments? The wisdom, understanding, non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view concerning rising from those attainments – this is called skill in rising from attainments.
^1-1339

What is skill in elements? The eighteen elements: eye element, form element, eye-consciousness element; ear element, sound element, ear-consciousness element; nose element, smell element, nose-consciousness element; tongue element, taste element, tongue-consciousness element; body element, tangible element, body-consciousness element; mind element, phenomena element, mind-consciousness element. The wisdom, understanding, non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view concerning those elements – this is called skill in elements.
^1-1340

What is skillfulness in attention?
That wisdom, understanding, non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view, which is skillfulness in attending to those elements—this is called skillfulness in attention.
^1-1341

What is skillfulness in sense bases?
The twelve sense bases: eye base, visible form base, ear base, sound base, nose base, smell base, tongue base, taste base, body base, tangible base, mind base, mental object base. That wisdom, understanding, non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view, which is skillfulness in these sense bases—this is called skillfulness in sense bases.
^1-1342

What is skillfulness in dependent origination?
Ignorance conditions formations; formations condition consciousness; consciousness conditions mind-and-matter; mind-and-matter conditions the six sense bases; the six sense bases condition contact; contact conditions feeling; feeling conditions craving; craving conditions clinging; clinging conditions existence; existence conditions birth; birth conditions decay-and-death, sorrow, lamentation, pain, displeasure, and despair. Thus arises this entire mass of suffering. That wisdom, understanding, non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view, concerning this—this is called skillfulness in dependent origination.
^1-1343

What is skillfulness in what is possible?
That which is the understanding that whatever phenomena are causes and conditions for the arising of whatever other phenomena, that is the possible. That wisdom, understanding, non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view, concerning this—this is called skillfulness in what is possible.
^1-1344

What is skillfulness in what is impossible?
That which is the understanding that whatever phenomena are not causes and conditions for the arising of whatever other phenomena, that is the impossible. That wisdom, understanding, non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view, concerning this—this is called skillfulness in what is impossible.
^1-1345

What is uprightness?
That which is straightforwardness, not being crooked, not being devious, not being dishonest—this is called uprightness.
^1-1346

What is gentleness?
That which is softness, gentleness, not harshness, not rigidity, humble-mindedness—this is called gentleness.
^1-1347

What is patience?
That which is patience, endurance, tolerance, absence of fierceness, absence of resentment, contentment of mind—this is called patience.
^1-1348

What is temperance in conduct?
That which is bodily non-transgression, verbal non-transgression, bodily and verbal non-transgression—this is called temperance in conduct. All moral restraint is temperance in conduct.
^1-1349

What is amiability in speech?
That speech which is harsh, rough, hurtful to others, abusive to others, bordering on anger, not conducive to concentration—having abandoned such speech, that speech which is gentle, pleasing to the ear, affectionate, heartwarming, courteous, agreeable to many, pleasant to many, is spoken; that which is polished speech, friendly speech, gentle speech—this is called amiability in speech.
^1-1350

What is hospitality?
There are two types of hospitality: material hospitality and Dharma hospitality. Here, some person is hospitable with material hospitality or Dharma hospitality—this is called hospitality.
^1-1351

What is unguardedness of the sense doors?
Here, perceiving forms with the eye, sounds with the ear, odors with the nose, flavors with the tongue, tangible objects with the body, or mental objects with the mind, one grasps at their general appearance and details. Whereby, while the respective faculty dwells unguarded, evil unwholesome states of covetousness and displeasure might flow in; one does not practice for its restraint, does not protect that faculty, does not undertake restraint in that faculty. The unguardedness, non-protection, non-guarding, non-restraint of these six faculties—this is called unguardedness of the sense doors.
^1-1352

What is immoderation in food?
Here, some person, without reflection, unskillfully takes food for sport, for intoxication, for adornment, for beautification. That dissatisfaction, immoderation, lack of reflection in food—this is called immoderation in food.
^1-1353

What is guardedness of the sense doors?
Here, perceiving forms with the eye, sounds with the ear, odors with the nose, flavors with the tongue, tangible objects with the body, or mental objects with the mind, one does not grasp at their general appearance nor details. Whereby, while the respective faculty dwells unguarded, evil unwholesome states of covetousness and displeasure might flow in; one practices for its restraint, protects that faculty, undertakes restraint in that faculty. The guarding, protection, restraint of these six faculties—this is called guardedness of the sense doors.
^1-1354

What is moderation in food?
Here, some person, with reflection, skillfully takes food—not for sport, not for intoxication, not for adornment, not for beautification, but solely for the maintenance and continuance of this body, for the cessation of discomfort, for the support of the holy life. "Thus I will end old feelings and not produce new feelings, and there will be sustenance for me, blamelessness, and comfortable living." That satisfaction, moderation, reflection in food—this is called moderation in food.
^1-1355

What is forgetfulness?
That which is absence of mindfulness, non-recollection, non-remembrance, inability to hold in mind, non-holding, letting slip, forgetting—this is called forgetfulness.
^1-1356

What is lack of clear comprehension?
That which is ignorance, non-seeing, delusion, the root of unwholesome states—this is called lack of clear comprehension.
^1-1357

What is mindfulness?
That which is mindfulness, recollection, remembrance, calling to mind, holding in mind, not letting slip, not forgetting; mindfulness, the faculty of mindfulness, the power of mindfulness, right mindfulness—this is called mindfulness.
^1-1358

What is clear comprehension?
That which is wisdom, understanding, non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view—this is called clear comprehension.
^1-1359

What is the power of reflection?
That which is wisdom, understanding, non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view—this is called the power of reflection.
^1-1360

What is the power of development? The repetition, development, and cultivation of wholesome states. This is called the power of development. The seven factors of enlightenment are also the power of development.
^1-1361

What is tranquility? The stability of mind...pe...right concentration. This is called tranquility.
^1-1362

What is insight? Wisdom, understanding...pe...non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view. This is called insight.
^1-1363

What is the sign of tranquility? The stability of mind...pe...right concentration. This is called the sign of tranquility.
^1-1364

What is the sign of exertion? Mental effort...pe...right effort. This is called the sign of exertion.
^1-1365

What is exertion? Mental effort...pe...right effort. This is called exertion.
^1-1366

What is non-distraction? The stability of mind...pe...right concentration. This is called non-distraction.
^1-1367

What is moral transgression? Bodily transgression, verbal transgression, or both bodily and verbal transgression. This is called moral transgression. All immorality is moral transgression.
^1-1368

What is wrong view?
There is no giving, no sacrifice, no offering; there is no fruit or result of good or evil deeds; there is no this world, nor the next; there is no mother, nor father; there are no spontaneously arisen beings; there are no recluses or brahmins in the world who have gone rightly, practiced rightly, who, having directly known and realized this world and the next by themselves, proclaim it. Such a view, wrong view...pe...perverted grasp. This is called wrong view. All wrong views are wrong view.
^1-1369

What is moral accomplishment? Bodily non-transgression, verbal non-transgression, or both bodily and verbal non-transgression. This is called moral accomplishment. All restraint in morality is moral accomplishment.
^1-1370

What is accomplishment in view? There is what is given, offered, sacrificed; there is fruit and result of good and bad actions; there is this world, there is the other world; there is mother, there is father; there are spontaneously arisen beings; there are in the world ascetics and brahmins who are rightly gone and rightly practiced, who proclaim this world and the other world, having directly known and realized them themselves. Such wisdom, knowing... non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view 
– this is called accomplishment in view. All right view is accomplishment in view.
^1-1371

What is purity of virtue? Bodily non-transgression, verbal non-transgression, bodily and verbal non-transgression 
– this is called purity of virtue. All virtuous restraint is purity of virtue.
^1-1372

What is purity of view? The knowledge that beings are owners of their actions, knowledge conforming to the Noble Truths, the wisdom of one endowed with the path, the wisdom of one endowed with the fruit.
^1-1373

Purity of view is that wisdom, knowing... non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view.
^1-1374

Exertion for one with such a view is mental arousal of energy... right effort.
^1-1375

Spiritual urgency is the fear of birth, the fear of old age, the fear of sickness, the fear of death. Grounds for spiritual urgency are birth, old age, sickness, death.
^1-1376

Proper exertion for one who has a sense of urgency: Here, a practitioner arouses desire, strives, exerts energy, grasps the mind, and strives for the non-arising of unarisen evil, unwholesome states; for the abandoning of arisen evil, unwholesome states; for the arising of unarisen wholesome states; for the maintenance, non-forgetting, increase, development, cultivation, and perfection of arisen wholesome states.
^1-1377

Non-contentment with wholesome states is the desire for more for one not content with the cultivation of wholesome states.
^1-1378

Non-recoiling from exertion is diligent performance, continuous performance, unwavering performance, unflagging conduct, unabandoned desire, unabandoned burden in the repetition, development, and cultivation of wholesome states.
^1-1379

True knowledge: The three true knowledges are the knowledge of recollecting past lives, the knowledge of the passing away and reappearing of beings, and the knowledge of the destruction of the taints.
^1-1380

Liberation means two liberations: the mind's resolution and Nibbāna.
^1-1381

Knowledge of destruction means the knowledge of one endowed with the path.
^1-1382

Knowledge of non-arising means the knowledge of one endowed with fruition.
The Section on Elucidation is concluded.
^1-1383

### 4. Commentary Section ^1-4-0

#### Elucidation of the Tika Meanings ^1-4-1-0

Which phenomena are wholesome? Wholesome phenomena are the wholesome in the four planes.
^1-1384

Which phenomena are unwholesome? Unwholesome phenomena are the twelve unwholesome states of consciousness.
^1-1385

Which phenomena are indeterminate? Indeterminate phenomena are resultant in the four planes, functional indeterminate in the three planes, and matter, and Nibbāna.
^1-1386

Which phenomena are associated with pleasant feeling? These phenomena are the four wholesome sense-sphere consciousnesses accompanied by joy; the four unwholesome (consciousnesses); the five resultant and functional from wholesome sense-sphere; the wholesome, resultant, and functional fine-material sphere 3rd and 4th jhānas; the wholesome and resultant supramundane 3rd and 4th jhānas; excluding the pleasant feeling that arises therein.
^1-1387

What phenomena are associated with painful feeling? The two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure, and the body-consciousness accompanied by pain, excluding the painful feeling arising therein 

– these phenomena are associated with painful feeling.
^1-1388

What phenomena are associated with neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling? The four wholesome sense-sphere consciousnesses accompanied by equanimity, the six unwholesome, the ten resultant wholesome sense-sphere, the six resultant unwholesome, the six functional, the fine-material sphere fourth jhána (wholesome, resultant, and functional), the four immaterial sphere (wholesome, resultant, and functional), and the supramundane fourth jhána (wholesome and resultant), excluding the neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling arising therein 

– these phenomena are associated with neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling. The three feelings, and matter, and Nibbána 

– these phenomena are not said to be associated with pleasant feeling, nor with painful feeling, nor with neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling.
^1-1389

What phenomena are resultants? Resultant phenomena in the four planes 

– these phenomena are resultants.
^1-1390

What phenomena are of the nature to produce resultants? Wholesome and unwholesome phenomena in the four planes 

– these phenomena are of the nature to produce resultants.
^1-1391

What phenomena are neither resultant nor of the nature to produce resultants? Functional indeterminate phenomena in the three planes, and matter, and Nibbána 

– these phenomena are neither resultant nor of the nature to produce resultants.
^1-1392

What phenomena are clung to and graspable? Resultant phenomena in the three planes, and that matter which is produced by kamma 

– these phenomena are clung to and graspable.
^1-1393

What phenomena are not clung to but graspable? Wholesome phenomena in the three planes, unwholesome phenomena in the three planes, functional indeterminate phenomena in the three planes, and that matter which is not produced by kamma 

– these phenomena are not clung to but graspable.
^1-1394

What phenomena are not clung to and not graspable? The four supramundane paths, the four fruitions of recluseship, and Nibbána 

– these phenomena are not clung to and not graspable.
^1-1395

What phenomena are defiled and defiling? The twelve unwholesome consciousnesses 

– these phenomena are defiled and defiling.
^1-1396

What phenomena are undefiled but defiling? Wholesome phenomena in the three planes, resultant phenomena in the three planes, functional indeterminate phenomena in the three planes, and all matter 

– these phenomena are undefiled but defiling.
^1-1397

What phenomena are undefiled and non-defiling? The four supramundane paths, the four fruits of recluseship, and Nibbána – these phenomena are undefiled and non-defiling.
^1-1398

What phenomena are accompanied by initial application and sustained application? Wholesome and unwholesome sense-sphere consciousnesses; eleven resultant wholesome sense-sphere consciousnesses; two resultant unwholesome consciousnesses; eleven functional consciousnesses; the first fine-material jhána, wholesome, resultant, and functional; the first supramundane jhána, wholesome and resultant – excluding the initial application and sustained application arising therein – these phenomena are accompanied by initial application and sustained application.
^1-1399

What phenomena are without initial application but with sustained application only? The second fine-material jhána in the five-fold system, wholesome, resultant, and functional; the second supramundane jhána in the five-fold system, wholesome and resultant – excluding the sustained application arising therein – and initial application – these phenomena are without initial application but with sustained application only.
^1-1400

What phenomena are without initial application and without sustained application? The two sets of fivefold sense consciousnesses; the fine-material jhána triads, wholesome, resultant, and functional; the four immaterial jhána consciousnesses, wholesome, resultant, and functional; the supramundane jhána triads, wholesome and resultant, and the second jhána in the five-fold system; and sustained application arising therein; and matter and Nibbána – these phenomena are without initial application and without sustained application. Sustained application co-arising with initial application should not be said to be accompanied by initial application and sustained application, nor only by sustained application, nor without initial application and without sustained application.
^1-1401

What phenomena are accompanied by rapture? Four wholesome sense-sphere consciousnesses accompanied by joy; four unwholesome consciousnesses; five resultant wholesome sense-sphere consciousnesses; five functional consciousnesses; the fine-material jhána dyads and triads, wholesome, resultant, and functional; the supramundane jhána dyads and triads, wholesome and resultant – excluding the rapture arising therein – these phenomena are accompanied by rapture.
^1-1402

What phenomena are accompanied by happiness? Four wholesome sense-sphere consciousnesses accompanied by joy; four unwholesome consciousnesses; six resultant wholesome sense-sphere consciousnesses; five functional consciousnesses; the fine-material jhána triads and tetrads, wholesome, resultant, and functional; the supramundane jhána triads and tetrads, wholesome and resultant – excluding the happiness arising therein – these phenomena are accompanied by happiness.
^1-1403

What phenomena are accompanied by equanimity? Four wholesome sense-sphere consciousnesses accompanied by equanimity; six unwholesome consciousnesses; ten resultant wholesome sense-sphere consciousnesses; six resultant unwholesome consciousnesses; six functional consciousnesses; the fourth fine-material jhána, wholesome, resultant, and functional; the four immaterial jhána consciousnesses, wholesome, resultant, and functional; the fourth supramundane jhána, wholesome and resultant – excluding the equanimity arising therein – these phenomena are accompanied by equanimity. Rapture is not accompanied by rapture, is accompanied by happiness, is not accompanied by equanimity. Happiness is not accompanied by happiness, may be accompanied by rapture, is not accompanied by equanimity, and may not be said to be accompanied by rapture. The two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure, body-consciousness accompanied by pain, the feeling of equanimity, and matter and Nibbána – these phenomena should not be said to be accompanied by rapture, nor by happiness, nor by equanimity.
^1-1404

What phenomena are to be abandoned by insight? The four consciousnesses associated with wrong view, and the consciousness accompanied by doubt – these phenomena are to be abandoned by insight.
^1-1405

What phenomena are to be abandoned by development? The consciousness accompanied by restlessness – these phenomena are to be abandoned by development. The four greed-rooted consciousnesses dissociated from wrong view, and the two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure – these phenomena may be abandoned by insight or by development.
^1-1406

What phenomena are to be abandoned neither by insight nor by development? Wholesome consciousnesses in the four planes; resultant consciousnesses in the four planes; functional indeterminate consciousnesses in the three planes; and matter and Nibbána – these phenomena are to be abandoned neither by insight nor by development.
^1-1407

Which states have roots to be abandoned by seeing? The four consciousnesses associated with wrong view, the consciousness accompanied by doubt, excluding the delusion arising therein – these states have roots to be abandoned by seeing.
^1-1408

Which states have roots to be abandoned by development? The consciousness accompanied by restlessness, excluding the delusion arising therein – these states have roots to be abandoned by development. The four consciousnesses accompanied by greed, dissociated from wrong view, and the two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure – these states may have roots to be abandoned by seeing, or may have roots to be abandoned by development.
^1-1409

Which states have roots to be abandoned neither by seeing nor by development? Delusion accompanied by doubt, delusion accompanied by restlessness, wholesome states in the four planes, resultant states in the four planes, functional indeterminate states in the three planes, and matter, and Nibbāna – these states have roots to be abandoned neither by seeing nor by development.
^1-1410

Which states lead to accumulation? Wholesome states in the three planes, unwholesome states – these states lead to accumulation.
^1-1411

Which states lead to reduction? The four supramundane paths – these states lead to reduction.
^1-1412

Which states lead neither to accumulation nor to reduction? Resultant states in the four planes, functional indeterminate states in the three planes, and matter, and Nibbāna – these states lead neither to accumulation nor to reduction.
^1-1413

Which states are learners? The four supramundane paths, and the three lower fruits of recluseship – these states are learners.
^1-1414

Which states are perfected? The highest fruit of Arahantship – these states are perfected.
^1-1415

Which states are neither learners nor perfected? Wholesome states in the three planes, unwholesome states, resultant states in the three planes, functional indeterminate states in the three planes, and matter, and Nibbāna – these states are neither learners nor perfected.
^1-1416

Which states are limited? Wholesome states of the sense sphere, unwholesome states, all resultant states of the sense sphere, functional indeterminate states of the sense sphere, and all matter – these states are limited.
^1-1417

What dhammas are exalted? Form-sphere, formless-sphere, wholesome-indeterminate – these dhammas are exalted.
^1-1418

What dhammas are immeasurable? The four paths not included (in the realms), the four fruitions of recluseship, and Nibbāna – these dhammas are immeasurable.
^1-1419

What dhammas have limited objects? All resultant consciousness of the sense-sphere, functional mind-element, functional rootless mind-consciousness element accompanied by joy – these dhammas have limited objects.
^1-1420

What dhammas have exalted objects? The base of infinite consciousness, the base of neither perception nor non-perception – these dhammas have exalted objects.
^1-1421

What dhammas have immeasurable objects? The four paths not included (in the realms), the four fruitions of recluseship – these dhammas have immeasurable objects. From wholesome sense-sphere consciousness, the four consciousnesses dissociated from knowledge; from functional consciousness, the four consciousnesses dissociated from knowledge; all unwholesome (consciousness) – these dhammas may have limited objects, may have exalted objects, not immeasurable objects, and may not be describable as having limited objects or as having exalted objects. From wholesome sense-sphere consciousness, the four consciousnesses associated with knowledge; from functional consciousness, the four consciousnesses associated with knowledge; the fourth rūpa-jhāna, both wholesome and functional; functional rootless mind-consciousness element accompanied by equanimity – these dhammas may have limited objects, may have exalted objects, may have immeasurable objects, and may not be describable as having limited objects, or as having exalted objects, or as having immeasurable objects. The rūpa-jhānas of the triple and quadruple systems, wholesome, resultant, and functional; the resultant of the fourth jhāna; the base of infinite space; the base of nothingness – these dhammas are not describable as having limited objects, or as having exalted objects, or as having immeasurable objects. Matter and Nibbāna are without object.
^1-1422

What dhammas are inferior? The twelve unwholesome consciousnesses – these dhammas are inferior.
^1-1423

What dhammas are middling? Wholesome (consciousness) in the three planes, resultant (consciousness) in the three planes, functional indeterminate (consciousness) in the three planes, and all matter – these dhammas are middling.
^1-1424

What dhammas are superior? The four paths not included (in the realms), the four fruitions of recluseship, and Nibbāna – these dhammas are superior.
^1-1425

What dhammas are fixed in wrongness? The four consciousnesses associated with wrong view, the two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure – these dhammas may be fixed in wrongness, may be unfixed.
^1-1426

What dhammas are fixed in rightness? The four paths not included (in the realms) – these dhammas are fixed in rightness.
^1-1427

Which phenomena are indeterminate? The four consciousnesses accompanied by greed dissociated from wrong view, consciousness accompanied by doubt, consciousness accompanied by restlessness, wholesome (consciousness) in the three planes, resultant (consciousness) in the four planes, functional indeterminate (consciousness) in the three planes, matter, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are indeterminate.
^1-1428

Which phenomena are path-objects? The four wisdom-associated wholesome consciousnesses of the sense sphere, and the four wisdom-associated functional consciousnesses – these phenomena may be path-objects, are not path-causes; they may be path-dominant, or cannot be described as path-objects or path-dominant. The four Noble Paths are not path-objects, are path-causes; they may be path-dominant, or cannot be described as path-dominant. The fourth fine-material jhána, wholesome and functional, and the functional rootless mind-consciousness element accompanied by equanimity – these phenomena may be path-objects; are not path-causes, are not path-dominant; or cannot be described as path-objects. The four wisdom-dissociated wholesome consciousnesses of the sense sphere, all unwholesome (consciousness), all resultant (consciousness) of the sense sphere, the six functional consciousnesses, the three and fourfold fine-material jhānas, wholesome, resultant, and functional, the resultant of the fourth jhána, the four immaterial (attainments), wholesome, resultant, and functional, and the four fruits of recluseship – these phenomena cannot be described as path-objects, path-causes, or path-dominant. Matter and Nibbāna are objectless.
^1-1429

Which phenomena are arisen? Resultant (consciousness) in the four planes and matter born of kamma – these phenomena may be arisen, may be arising; cannot be described as unarisen. Wholesome (consciousness) and unwholesome (consciousness) in the four planes, functional indeterminate (consciousness) in the three planes, and matter not born of kamma – these phenomena may be arisen, may be unarisen; cannot be described as arising. Nibbāna cannot be described as arisen, unarisen, or arising.
^1-1430

Except Nibbāna, all phenomena may be past, may be future, may be present. Nibbāna cannot be described as past, future, or present.
^1-1431

Which phenomena have past objects? The base of infinite consciousness and the base of neither perception nor non-perception – these phenomena have past objects.
^1-1432

Necessarily, there are no future objects.
^1-1433

Which phenomena have present objects? The two sets of fivefold sense consciousnesses and the three mind-elements – these phenomena have present objects. The ten resultant consciousnesses of the sense sphere, the resultant mind-consciousness element accompanied by equanimity of the unwholesome, the functional rootless mind-consciousness element accompanied by joy – these phenomena may have past objects, may have future objects, may have present objects. Wholesome (consciousness) of the sense sphere, unwholesome (consciousness), the nine functional consciousnesses, the fourth fine-material jhána, wholesome and functional – these phenomena may have past objects, may have future objects, may have present objects; or cannot be described as having past, future, or present objects. The three and fourfold fine-material jhānas, wholesome, resultant, and functional, the resultant of the fourth jhána, the base of infinite space, the base of nothingness, the four supramundane paths, and the four fruits of recluseship – these phenomena cannot be described as having past, future, or present objects. Matter and Nibbāna are objectless.
^1-1434

Except matter not bound to faculties and Nibbāna, all phenomena may be internal, may be external, may be internal and external. Matter not bound to faculties and Nibbāna are external.
^1-1435

Which phenomena have internal objects? The base of infinite consciousness and the base of neither perception nor non-perception – these phenomena have internal objects.
^1-1436

Which phenomena have external objects? The three and fourfold fine-material jhānas, wholesome, resultant, and functional, the resultant of the fourth jhána, the base of infinite space, the four supramundane paths, and the four fruits of recluseship – these phenomena have external objects. Except matter, all wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate phenomena of the sense sphere, and the fourth fine-material jhána, wholesome and functional – these phenomena may have internal objects, may have external objects, may have internal and external objects. The base of nothingness cannot be described as having internal, external, or internal and external objects. Matter and Nibbāna are objectless.
^1-1437

What are the phenomena that are visible and with resistance? The visible object – these phenomena are visible and with resistance.
^1-1438

What are the phenomena that are invisible but with resistance? The eye-base... the tangible object – these phenomena are invisible but with resistance.
^1-1439

What are the phenomena that are invisible and without resistance? Wholesome in the four planes, unwholesome, resultant in the four planes, functional in the three planes, and that matter which is invisible and without resistance and is included in the dharma-base, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are invisible and without resistance.
Triad.
^1-1440

#### Elucidation of Dyads ^1-4-2-0

##### Chapter on Roots ^1-4-2-1-0

What are the phenomena that are roots? The three wholesome roots, the three unwholesome roots, the three indeterminate roots. Non-greed is a wholesome root, non-hatred is a wholesome root, they arise in wholesome states in the four planes. Non-delusion is a wholesome root, it arises in wholesome states in the four planes, excluding the four consciousnesses dissociated from knowledge in sensuous wholesome states.
 Greed arises in the eight consciousnesses accompanied by greed. Hatred arises in the two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure. Delusion arises in all unwholesome states.
 Non-greed is a resultant root, non-hatred is a resultant root, they arise in resultant states in the four planes, excluding the rootless consciousnesses of the sensuous resultant. Non-delusion is a resultant root, it arises in resultant states in the four planes, excluding the rootless consciousnesses of the sensuous resultant, and excluding the four consciousnesses dissociated from knowledge.
 Non-greed is a functional root, non-hatred is a functional root, they arise in functional states in the three planes, excluding the rootless consciousnesses of the sensuous functional. Non-delusion is a functional root, it arises in functional states in the three planes, excluding the rootless consciousnesses of the sensuous functional, and excluding the four consciousnesses dissociated from knowledge – these phenomena are roots.
^1-1441

What are the phenomena that are not roots? Excluding the roots, wholesome in the four planes, unwholesome, resultant in the four planes, functional in the three planes, and matter, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are not roots.
^1-1442

What are the phenomena that are associated with roots? The remaining unwholesome states, excluding the delusion accompanied by doubt and the delusion accompanied by restlessness; wholesome in the four planes; resultant in the four planes, excluding the rootless consciousnesses of the sensuous resultant; functional in the three planes, excluding the rootless consciousnesses of the sensuous functional – these phenomena are associated with roots.
^1-1443

What are the phenomena that are rootless? The delusion accompanied by doubt, the delusion accompanied by restlessness, the two five-fold consciousnesses, the three mind-elements, the five rootless mind-consciousness-elements, and matter, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are rootless.
^1-1444

Which phenomena are associated with roots? Excluding delusion accompanied by doubt and delusion accompanied by restlessness, the remaining unwholesome; wholesome in the four planes; resultant in the four planes, excluding rootless consciousness of the sense sphere; functional indeterminate in the three planes, excluding rootless consciousness of the sense sphere – these phenomena are associated with roots.
^1-1445

Which phenomena are dissociated from roots? Delusion accompanied by doubt, delusion accompanied by restlessness, the two sets of fivefold sense consciousness, the three mind-elements, the five rootless mind-consciousness elements, matter, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are dissociated from roots.
^1-1446

Which phenomena are roots and are rooted? Where two or three roots arise together – these phenomena are roots and are rooted.
^1-1447

Which phenomena are rooted but are not roots? Wholesome in the four planes, unwholesome, resultant in the four planes, excluding rootless consciousness of the sense sphere; functional indeterminate in the three planes, excluding rootless consciousness of the sense sphere, excluding the roots that arise therein – these phenomena are rooted but are not roots. Rootless phenomena should not be called "roots and rooted" nor "rooted but not roots."
^1-1448

Which phenomena are roots and are associated with roots? Where two or three roots arise together – these phenomena are roots and are associated with roots.
^1-1449

Which phenomena are associated with roots but are not roots? Wholesome in the four planes, unwholesome, resultant in the four planes, excluding rootless consciousness of the sense sphere; functional indeterminate in the three planes, excluding rootless consciousness of the sense sphere, excluding the roots that arise therein – these phenomena are associated with roots but are not roots. Phenomena dissociated from roots should not be called "roots and associated with roots" nor "associated with roots but not roots."
^1-1450

Which phenomena are not roots but are rooted? Wholesome in the four planes, unwholesome, resultant in the four planes, excluding rootless consciousness of the sense sphere; functional indeterminate in the three planes, excluding rootless consciousness of the sense sphere, excluding the roots that arise therein – these phenomena are not roots but are rooted.
^1-1451

Which phenomena are not roots and are rootless? The two sets of fivefold sense consciousness, the three mind-elements, the five rootless mind-consciousness elements, matter, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are not roots and are rootless. Roots should not be called "not roots but rooted" nor "not roots and rootless."
^1-1452

##### Minor Intermediate Dyad ^1-4-2-2-0

Which phenomena are with conditions? Wholesome in the four planes, unwholesome, resultant in the four planes, functional indeterminate in the three planes, and all matter – these phenomena are with conditions.
^1-1453

What are the unconditioned phenomena? Nibbana – these are the unconditioned phenomena.
^1-1454

What are the conditioned phenomena? Wholesome in the four planes, unwholesome, resultant in the four planes, functional in the three planes, and all matter – these are the conditioned phenomena.
^1-1455

What are the unconditioned phenomena? Nibbana – these are the unconditioned phenomena.
^1-1456

What are the visible phenomena? The visible object – these are the visible phenomena.
^1-1457

What are the invisible phenomena? The eye-base ...pe... the tangible-base, wholesome in the four planes, unwholesome, resultant in the four planes, functional in the three planes, and that matter which is invisible, non-impinging, included in the dhamma-base, and Nibbana – these are the invisible phenomena.
^1-1458

What are the impinging phenomena? The eye-base ...pe... the tangible-base – these are the impinging phenomena.
^1-1459

What are the non-impinging phenomena? Wholesome in the four planes, unwholesome, resultant in the four planes, functional in the three planes, and that matter which is invisible, non-impinging, included in the dhamma-base, and Nibbana – these are the non-impinging phenomena.
^1-1460

What are the material phenomena? The four great primaries, and derivative matter dependent on the four great primaries – these are the material phenomena.
^1-1461

What are the immaterial phenomena? Wholesome in the four planes, unwholesome, resultant in the four planes, functional in the three planes, and Nibbana – these are the immaterial phenomena.
^1-1462

What are the mundane phenomena? Wholesome in the three planes, unwholesome, resultant in the three planes, functional in the three planes, and all matter – these are the mundane phenomena.
^1-1463

What are the transcendent states? The four supramundane paths, the four fruition states, and Nibbāna 

– these states are transcendent. All states are knowable by some, not knowable by some.
^1-1464

##### The Section on Taints ^1-4-2-3-0

What are the taints? The four taints: taint of sensual desire, taint of existence, taint of views, taint of ignorance. The taint of sensual desire arises in the eight consciousnesses accompanied by greed. The taint of existence arises in the four consciousnesses accompanied by greed dissociated from wrong view. The taint of views arises in the four consciousnesses associated with wrong view. The taint of ignorance arises in all unwholesome states. These states are taints.
^1-1465

What are not taints? Excluding the taints, the remaining unwholesome states, wholesome states in the four planes, resultant states in the four planes, functional indeterminate states in the three planes, matter, and Nibbāna – these states are not taints.
^1-1466

What are tainted states? Wholesome states in the three planes, unwholesome states, resultant states in the three planes, functional indeterminate states in the three planes, and all matter – these states are tainted.
^1-1467

What are untainted states? The four supramundane paths, the four fruition states, and Nibbāna – these states are untainted.
^1-1468

What are states concomitant with taints? Excluding the delusion arising in the two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure, excluding the delusion accompanied by doubt and restlessness, the remaining unwholesome states – these states are concomitant with taints.
^1-1469

What are states dissociated from taints? The delusion arising in the two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure, the delusion accompanied by doubt, the delusion accompanied by restlessness, wholesome states in the four planes, resultant states in the four planes, functional indeterminate states in the three planes, matter, and Nibbāna – these states are dissociated from taints.
^1-1470

What are states that are both taints and tainted? Those same taints are both taints and tainted.
^1-1471

What are states that are tainted but not taints? Excluding the taints, the remaining unwholesome states, wholesome states in the three planes, resultant states in the three planes, functional indeterminate states in the three planes, and all matter – these states are tainted but not taints. Untainted states are not to be called “both taints and tainted” nor “tainted but not taints.”
^1-1472

Which phenomena are both cankers and associated with cankers? Where two or three cankers arise together – these phenomena are both cankers and associated with cankers.
^1-1473

Which phenomena are associated with cankers but are not cankers themselves? Excluding the cankers, the remaining unwholesome phenomena – these phenomena are associated with cankers but are not cankers themselves. Phenomena disjoined from cankers are not to be called 'both cankers and associated with cankers' nor 'associated with cankers but not cankers themselves'.
^1-1474

Which phenomena are disjoined from cankers but still have cankers? Ignorance arising in the two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure, ignorance accompanied by doubt, ignorance accompanied by restlessness, wholesome phenomena in the three planes, resultant phenomena in the three planes, functional phenomena in the three planes, and all material phenomena – these phenomena are disjoined from cankers but still have cankers.
^1-1475

Which phenomena are disjoined from cankers and are without cankers? The four supramundane paths, the four supramundane fruits, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are disjoined from cankers and are without cankers. Phenomena associated with cankers are not to be called 'disjoined from cankers but having cankers' nor 'disjoined from cankers and without cankers'.
^1-1476

##### Saṃyojanagocchakaṃ ^1-4-2-4-0

Which phenomena are fetters? The ten fetters: sensual craving, ill-will, conceit, wrong view, doubt, adherence to rites and rituals, craving for existence, envy, avarice, ignorance. Sensual craving arises in the eight greed-rooted consciousnesses. Ill-will arises in the two hate-rooted consciousnesses. Conceit arises in the four greed-rooted consciousnesses disjoined from wrong view. Wrong view arises in the four greed-rooted consciousnesses conjoined with wrong view. Doubt arises in the doubt-rooted consciousness. Adherence to rites and rituals arises in the four greed-rooted consciousnesses conjoined with wrong view. Craving for existence arises in the four greed-rooted consciousnesses disjoined from wrong view. Envy and avarice arise in the two hate-rooted consciousnesses. Ignorance arises in all unwholesome consciousnesses – these phenomena are fetters.
^1-1477

Which phenomena are not fetters? Excluding the fetters, the remaining unwholesome phenomena, wholesome phenomena in the four planes, resultant phenomena in the four planes, functional phenomena in the three planes, material phenomena, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are not fetters.
^1-1478

Which phenomena are capable of being fettered? Wholesome phenomena in the three planes, unwholesome phenomena, resultant phenomena in the three planes, functional phenomena in the three planes, and all material phenomena – these phenomena are capable of being fettered.
^1-1479

Which phenomena are not capable of being fettered? The four supramundane paths, the four supramundane fruits, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are not capable of being fettered.
^1-1480

Which phenomena are associated with fetters? Excluding ignorance accompanied by restlessness, the remaining unwholesome phenomena – these phenomena are associated with fetters.
^1-1481

Which dhammas are disjoined from fetters? Delusion accompanied by restlessness, wholesome consciousness in the four planes, resultant consciousness in the four planes, functional indeterminate consciousness in the three planes, matter, and Nibbana – these dhammas are disjoined from fetters.
^1-1482

Which dhammas are both fetters and conducive to fetters? The fetters themselves are both fetters and conducive to fetters.
^1-1483

Which dhammas are conducive to fetters but are not fetters? Excluding the fetters, the remaining unwholesome dhammas, wholesome consciousness in the three planes, resultant consciousness in the three planes, functional indeterminate consciousness in the three planes, and all matter – these dhammas are conducive to fetters but are not fetters. Dhammas not conducive to fetters should not be called "both fetters and conducive to fetters" nor "conducive to fetters but not fetters."
^1-1484

Which dhammas are both fetters and conjoined with fetters? Where two or three fetters arise together – these dhammas are both fetters and conjoined with fetters.
^1-1485

Which dhammas are conjoined with fetters but are not fetters? Excluding the fetters, the remaining unwholesome dhammas – these dhammas are conjoined with fetters but are not fetters. Dhammas disjoined from fetters should not be called "both fetters and conjoined with fetters" nor "conjoined with fetters but not fetters."
^1-1486

Which dhammas are disjoined from fetters but conducive to fetters? Delusion accompanied by restlessness, wholesome consciousness in the three planes, resultant consciousness in the three planes, functional indeterminate consciousness in the three planes, and all matter – these dhammas are disjoined from fetters but conducive to fetters.
^1-1487

Which dhammas are disjoined from fetters and not conducive to fetters? The four supramundane paths, the four fruits of recluseship, and Nibbana – these dhammas are disjoined from fetters and not conducive to fetters. Dhammas conjoined with fetters should not be called "disjoined from fetters but conducive to fetters" nor "disjoined from fetters and not conducive to fetters."
^1-1488

##### Group of Bodily Ties ^1-4-2-5-0

Which dhammas are ties? There are four ties: covetousness bodily tie, ill-will bodily tie, attachment to rites and rituals bodily tie, and dogmatic adherence to 'this is the truth' bodily tie. The covetousness bodily tie arises in the eight consciousnesses accompanied by greed. The ill-will bodily tie arises in the two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure. The attachment to rites and rituals bodily tie and the dogmatic adherence to 'this is the truth' bodily tie arise in the four consciousnesses conjoined with wrong view – these dhammas are ties.
^1-1489

Which dhammas are not ties? Excluding the ties, the remaining unwholesome dhammas, wholesome consciousness in the four planes, resultant consciousness in the four planes, functional indeterminate consciousness in the three planes, matter, and Nibbana – these dhammas are not ties.
^1-1490

Which phenomena are tied? Wholesome, unwholesome, resultant, and functional states in the three planes, and all matter – these phenomena are tied.
^1-1491

Which phenomena are untied? The four supramundane paths, the four fruits of recluseship, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are untied.
^1-1492

Which phenomena are associated with ties? The four consciousnesses accompanied by wrong view, the four greed-rooted consciousnesses dissociated from wrong view (excluding the greed arising therein), and the two displeasure-accompanied consciousnesses (excluding the aversion arising therein) – these phenomena are associated with ties.
^1-1493

Which phenomena are dissociated from ties? The greed arising in the four greed-rooted consciousnesses dissociated from wrong view, the aversion arising in the two displeasure-accompanied consciousnesses, the consciousness accompanied by doubt, the consciousness accompanied by restlessness, wholesome states in the four planes, resultant states in the four planes, functional states in the three planes, matter, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are dissociated from ties.
^1-1494

Which phenomena are both ties and tied? The ties themselves are both ties and tied.
^1-1495

Which phenomena are tied but not ties? The remaining unwholesome states (excluding the ties), wholesome states in the three planes, resultant states in the three planes, functional states in the three planes, and all matter – these phenomena are tied but not ties. Untied phenomena are not to be called "both ties and tied" nor "tied but not ties."
^1-1496

Which phenomena are both ties and associated with ties? Where wrong view and greed arise together – these phenomena are both ties and associated with ties.
^1-1497

Which phenomena are associated with ties but not ties? The eight greed-rooted consciousnesses and the two displeasure-accompanied consciousnesses, excluding the ties arising therein – these phenomena are associated with ties but not ties. Phenomena dissociated from ties are not to be called "both ties and associated with ties" nor "associated with ties but not ties."
^1-1498

Which phenomena are dissociated from ties and tied? The greed arising in the four greed-rooted consciousnesses dissociated from wrong view, the aversion arising in the two displeasure-accompanied consciousnesses, the consciousness accompanied by doubt, the consciousness accompanied by restlessness, wholesome states in the three planes, resultant states in the three planes, functional states in the three planes, and all matter – these phenomena are dissociated from ties and tied.
^1-1499

Which phenomena are dissociated from ties and untied? The four supramundane paths, the four fruits of recluseship, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are dissociated from ties and untied. Phenomena associated with ties are not to be called "dissociated from ties and tied" nor "dissociated from ties and untied."
^1-1500

##### The Floods Group ^1-4-2-6-0

What are the phenomena that are floods...pe....
^1-1501

##### The Yokes Group ^1-4-2-7-0

What are the phenomena that are yokes...pe....
^1-1502

##### The Hindrances Group ^1-4-2-8-0

What are the phenomena that are hindrances? Six hindrances: sensual desire hindrance, ill will hindrance, sloth and torpor hindrance, restlessness and worry hindrance, skeptical doubt hindrance, ignorance hindrance. Sensual desire hindrance arises in the eight consciousnesses accompanied by greed; ill will hindrance arises in the two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure; sloth and torpor hindrance arises in prompted unwholesome states; restlessness hindrance arises in consciousnesses accompanied by restlessness; worry hindrance arises in the two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure; skeptical doubt hindrance arises in consciousnesses accompanied by skeptical doubt; ignorance hindrance arises in all unwholesome states. These phenomena are hindrances.
^1-1503

What are the phenomena that are not hindrances? Apart from the hindrances, the remaining unwholesome states, wholesome states in the four planes, resultant states in the four planes, functional indeterminate states in the three planes, and matter, and Nibbāna. These phenomena are not hindrances.
^1-1504

What are the phenomena that are hindrable? Wholesome states in the three planes, unwholesome states, resultant states in the three planes, functional indeterminate states in the three planes, and all matter. These phenomena are hindrable.
^1-1505

What are the phenomena that are unhindrable? The four supramundane paths, and the four fruits of recluseship, and Nibbāna. These phenomena are unhindrable.
^1-1506

What are the phenomena associated with hindrances? The twelve unwholesome consciousnesses. These phenomena are associated with hindrances.
^1-1507

Which dhammas are dissociated from hindrances? Wholesome consciousness in the four planes, resultant consciousness in the four planes, functional consciousness in the three planes, and matter, and Nibbāna – these dhammas are dissociated from hindrances.
^1-1508

Which dhammas are hindrances and also liable to be hindered? The hindrances themselves are hindrances and also liable to be hindered.
^1-1509

Which dhammas are liable to be hindered but are not hindrances themselves? Excluding the hindrances, the remaining unwholesome states, wholesome consciousness in the three planes, resultant consciousness in the three planes, functional consciousness in the three planes, and all matter – these dhammas are liable to be hindered but are not hindrances themselves. Dhammas not liable to be hindered should not be said to be hindrances and also liable to be hindered, or liable to be hindered but not hindrances themselves.
^1-1510

Which dhammas are hindrances and associated with hindrances? Where two or three hindrances arise together – these dhammas are hindrances and associated with hindrances.
^1-1511

Which dhammas are associated with hindrances but are not hindrances themselves? Excluding the hindrances, the remaining unwholesome states – these dhammas are associated with hindrances but are not hindrances themselves. Dhammas dissociated from hindrances should not be said to be hindrances and associated with hindrances, or associated with hindrances but not hindrances themselves.
^1-1512

Which dhammas are dissociated from hindrances but liable to be hindered? Wholesome consciousness in the three planes, resultant consciousness in the three planes, functional consciousness in the three planes, and all matter – these dhammas are dissociated from hindrances but liable to be hindered.
^1-1513

Which dhammas are dissociated from hindrances and not liable to be hindered? The four supramundane paths, and the four supramundane fruits, and Nibbāna – these dhammas are dissociated from hindrances and not liable to be hindered. Dhammas associated with hindrances should not be said to be dissociated from hindrances but liable to be hindered, or dissociated from hindrances and not liable to be hindered.
^1-1514

##### Group concerning Misapprehension/Clinging ^1-4-2-9-0

Which dhammas are misapprehensions/clinging? Wrong view arises in the four types of consciousness associated with wrong view – these dhammas are misapprehensions/clinging.
^1-1515

Which dhammas are not misapprehensions/clinging? Excluding misapprehension/clinging, the remaining unwholesome states, wholesome consciousness in the four planes, resultant consciousness in the four planes, functional consciousness in the three planes, and matter, and Nibbāna – these dhammas are not misapprehensions/clinging.
^1-1516

Which phenomena are clung to? Wholesome, unwholesome, resultant, and functional indeterminate phenomena in the three realms, and all matter – these phenomena are clung to.
^1-1517

Which phenomena are not clung to? The four supramundane paths, the four supramundane fruits, and Nibbana – these phenomena are not clung to.
^1-1518

Which phenomena are associated with wrong view? The four consciousnesses associated with wrong view, excluding the wrong view arising therein – these phenomena are associated with wrong view.
^1-1519

Which phenomena are disassociated from wrong view? The four consciousnesses rooted in greed and disassociated from wrong view, the two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure, the consciousness accompanied by doubt, the consciousness accompanied by restlessness, wholesome and resultant phenomena in the four realms, functional indeterminate phenomena in the three realms, matter, and Nibbana – these phenomena are disassociated from wrong view. Wrong view itself is not to be classified as associated with wrong view, nor as disassociated from wrong view.
^1-1520

Which phenomena are wrong view and are clung to? Wrong view itself is wrong view and is clung to.
^1-1521

Which phenomena are clung to but are not wrong view? All unwholesome phenomena except wrong view, wholesome, resultant, and functional indeterminate phenomena in the three realms, and all matter – these phenomena are clung to but are not wrong view. Phenomena that are not clung to are not to be classified as wrong view and clung to, nor as clung to but not wrong view.
^1-1522

Which phenomena are disassociated from wrong view and are clung to? The four consciousnesses rooted in greed and disassociated from wrong view, the two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure, the consciousness accompanied by doubt, the consciousness accompanied by restlessness, wholesome, resultant, and functional indeterminate phenomena in the three realms, and all matter – these phenomena are disassociated from wrong view and are clung to.
^1-1523

Which phenomena are disassociated from wrong view and are not clung to? The four supramundane paths, the four supramundane fruits, and Nibbana – these phenomena are disassociated from wrong view and are not clung to. Wrong view itself and phenomena associated with wrong view are not to be classified as disassociated from wrong view and clung to, nor as disassociated from wrong view and not clung to.
^1-1524

##### Mahantaradukaṃ ^1-4-2-10-0

Which phenomena have an object? Wholesome, unwholesome, and resultant phenomena in the four realms, and functional indeterminate phenomena in the three realms – these phenomena have an object.
^1-1525

What phenomena are without object? Matter and Nibbāna – these phenomena are without object.
^1-1526

What phenomena are consciousness? Eye-consciousness, ear-consciousness, nose-consciousness, tongue-consciousness, body-consciousness, mind-element, mind-consciousness element – these phenomena are consciousness.
^1-1527

What phenomena are not consciousness? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations, matter, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are not consciousness.
^1-1528

What phenomena are mental factors? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations – these phenomena are mental factors.
^1-1529

What phenomena are not mental factors? Consciousness, matter, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are not mental factors.
^1-1530

What phenomena are associated with consciousness? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations – these phenomena are associated with consciousness.
^1-1531

What phenomena are dissociated from consciousness? Matter and Nibbāna – these phenomena are dissociated from consciousness. Consciousness should not be said to be associated with consciousness, nor dissociated from consciousness.
^1-1532

What phenomena are conjoined with consciousness? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations – these phenomena are conjoined with consciousness.
^1-1533

What phenomena are disjoined from consciousness? Matter and Nibbāna – these phenomena are disjoined from consciousness. Consciousness should not be said to be conjoined with consciousness, nor disjoined from consciousness.
^1-1534

What phenomena are originated by consciousness? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations, bodily intimation, verbal intimation, and whatever other matter there is that is mind-born, mind-caused, mind-originated – visual object, sound object, smell object, taste object, tangible object, space element, water element, lightness of matter, pliancy of matter, adaptability of matter, growth of matter, continuity of matter, nutriment – these phenomena are originated by consciousness.
^1-1535

What phenomena are not mind-originated? Consciousness, remaining matter, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are not mind-originated.
^1-1536

What phenomena are co-existent with mind? The feeling aggregate, the perception aggregate, the formations aggregate, bodily intimation, and verbal intimation – these phenomena are co-existent with mind.
^1-1537

What phenomena are not co-existent with mind? Consciousness, remaining matter, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are not co-existent with mind.
^1-1538

What phenomena accompany mind? The feeling aggregate, the perception aggregate, the formations aggregate, bodily intimation, and verbal intimation – these phenomena accompany mind.
^1-1539

What phenomena do not accompany mind? Consciousness, remaining matter, and Nibbāna – these phenomena do not accompany mind.
^1-1540

What phenomena originate from and are associated with mind? The feeling aggregate, the perception aggregate, and the formations aggregate – these phenomena originate from and are associated with mind.
^1-1541

What phenomena do not originate from and are associated with mind? Consciousness, matter, and Nibbāna – these phenomena do not originate from and are associated with mind.
^1-1542

What phenomena are co-existent with, originate from, and are associated with mind? The feeling aggregate, the perception aggregate, and the formations aggregate – these phenomena are co-existent with, originate from, and are associated with mind.
^1-1543

What phenomena are not co-existent with, originate from, and are associated with mind? Consciousness, matter, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are not co-existent with, originate from, and are associated with mind.
^1-1544

What phenomena accompany, originate from, and are associated with mind? The feeling aggregate, the perception aggregate, and the formations aggregate – these phenomena accompany, originate from, and are associated with mind.
^1-1545

Which phenomena do not arise in conjunction with, are not dependent on, and do not follow consciousness? Consciousness, matter, and Nibbana – these phenomena do not arise in conjunction with, are not dependent on, and do not follow consciousness.
^1-1546

Which phenomena are internal? The eye-base... up to... the mind-base – these phenomena are internal.
^1-1547

Which phenomena are external? The visible-form-base... up to... the dhamma-base – these phenomena are external.
^1-1548

Which phenomena are objects of clinging? The eye-base... up to... edible food – these phenomena are objects of clinging.
^1-1549

Which phenomena are not objects of clinging? Wholesome states in the four planes, unwholesome states, resultant states in the four planes, functional indeterminate states in the three planes, the four great essential matters, and Nibbana – these phenomena are not objects of clinging.
^1-1550

Which phenomena are clung-to? Resultant states in the three planes, and matter produced by kamma – these phenomena are clung-to.
^1-1551

Which phenomena are not clung-to? Wholesome states in the three planes, unwholesome states, functional indeterminate states in the three planes, matter not produced by kamma, the four supramundane paths, the four supramundane fruits, and Nibbana – these phenomena are not clung-to.
^1-1552

##### Upādānagocchakaṃ (The Section on Clinging) ^1-4-2-11-0

Which phenomena are clingings? The four clingings: sensual clinging, clinging to views, clinging to rites and rituals, clinging to a doctrine of self. Sensual clinging arises in the eight consciousnesses accompanied by greed. Clinging to views, clinging to rites and rituals, and clinging to a doctrine of self arise in the four consciousnesses associated with wrong view – these phenomena are clingings.
^1-1553
