Which phenomena are not clingings? Apart from the clingings, the remaining unwholesome states, wholesome states in the four planes, resultant states in the four planes, functional indeterminate states in the three planes, matter, and Nibbana – these phenomena are not clingings.
^1-1554

Which phenomena are objects of clinging? Wholesome states in the three planes, unwholesome states, resultant states in the three planes, functional states in the three planes, and all material phenomena – these phenomena are objects of clinging.
^1-1555

Which phenomena are not objects of clinging? The four supramundane paths, the four supramundane fruits, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are not objects of clinging.
^1-1556

Which phenomena are associated with clinging? The four consciousnesses rooted in greed associated with wrong view, and the four consciousnesses rooted in greed dissociated from wrong view, excluding the greed arising therein – these phenomena are associated with clinging.
^1-1557

Which phenomena are dissociated from clinging? The greed arising in the four consciousnesses rooted in greed dissociated from wrong view, the two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure, the consciousness accompanied by doubt, the consciousness accompanied by restlessness, wholesome states in the four planes, resultant states in the four planes, functional states in the three planes, material phenomena, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are dissociated from clinging.
^1-1558

Which phenomena are both clinging and objects of clinging? The clinging factors themselves are both clinging and objects of clinging.
^1-1559

Which phenomena are objects of clinging but not clinging itself? All remaining unwholesome states, excluding the clinging factors, wholesome states in the three planes, resultant states in the three planes, functional states in the three planes, and all material phenomena – these phenomena are objects of clinging but not clinging itself. Phenomena that are not objects of clinging cannot be said to be both clinging and objects of clinging, nor objects of clinging but not clinging itself.
^1-1560

Which phenomena are both clinging and associated with clinging? Where wrong view and greed arise together – these phenomena are both clinging and associated with clinging.
^1-1561

Which phenomena are associated with clinging but not clinging itself? The eight consciousnesses rooted in greed, excluding the clinging factors arising therein – these phenomena are associated with clinging but not clinging itself. Phenomena dissociated from clinging cannot be said to be both clinging and associated with clinging, nor associated with clinging but not clinging itself.
^1-1562

Which phenomena are dissociated from clinging but are objects of clinging? The greed arising in the four consciousnesses rooted in greed dissociated from wrong view, the two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure, the consciousness accompanied by doubt, the consciousness accompanied by restlessness, wholesome states in the three planes, resultant states in the three planes, functional states in the three planes, and all material phenomena – these phenomena are dissociated from clinging but are objects of clinging.
^1-1563

Which phenomena are dissociated from clinging and are not objects of clinging? The four supramundane paths, the four supramundane fruits, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are dissociated from clinging and are not objects of clinging. Phenomena associated with clinging cannot be said to be dissociated from clinging but objects of clinging, nor dissociated from clinging and not objects of clinging.
^1-1564

##### The Section on Defilements ^1-4-2-12-0

What are defilements? The ten bases of defilements: greed, hatred, delusion, conceit, wrong view, doubt, sloth, restlessness, shamelessness, fearlessness of wrongdoing. Greed arises in the eight consciousnesses accompanied by greed. Hatred arises in the two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure. Delusion arises in all unwholesome states. Conceit arises in the four consciousnesses accompanied by greed, dissociated from wrong view. Wrong view arises in the four consciousnesses associated with wrong view. Doubt arises in the consciousness accompanied by doubt. Sloth arises in prompted unwholesome states. Restlessness, shamelessness, and fearlessness of wrongdoing arise in all unwholesome states. These states are defilements.
^1-1565

What are not defilements? Excluding defilements, the remaining unwholesome states, wholesome states in the four planes, resultant states in the four planes, functional indeterminate states in the three planes, matter, and Nibbāna. These states are not defilements.
^1-1566

What are defiling things? Wholesome states in the three planes, unwholesome states, resultant states in the three planes, functional indeterminate states in the three planes, and all matter. These states are defiling.
^1-1567

What are non-defiling things? The four supramundane paths, the four fruition states, and Nibbāna. These states are non-defiling.
^1-1568

What are defiled things? The twelve unwholesome consciousnesses. These states are defiled.
^1-1569

What are undefiled things? Wholesome states in the four planes, resultant states in the four planes, functional indeterminate states in the three planes, matter, and Nibbāna. These states are undefiled.
^1-1570

What are associated with defilements? The twelve unwholesome consciousnesses. These states are associated with defilements.
^1-1571

What are dissociated from defilements? Wholesome states in the four planes, resultant states in the four planes, functional indeterminate states in the three planes, matter, and Nibbāna. These states are dissociated from defilements.
^1-1572

What are defilements and defiling? The defilements themselves are defilements and defiling.
^1-1573

Which dhammas are defiling but not defilements? All unwholesome states except the defilements; wholesome, resultant, and functional indeterminate states in the three planes; and all material phenomena. These dhammas are defiling but not defilements. Undefiling dhammas are not to be called defilements and defiling, nor defiling but not defilements.
^1-1574

Which dhammas are defilements and defiled? The defilements themselves are defilements and defiled.
^1-1575

Which dhammas are defiled but not defilements? All unwholesome states except the defilements. These dhammas are defiled but not defilements. Undefiled dhammas are not to be called defilements and defiled, nor defiled but not defilements.
^1-1576

Which dhammas are defilements and associated with defilements? Where two or three defilements arise together. These dhammas are defilements and associated with defilements.
^1-1577

Which dhammas are associated with defilements but not defilements? All unwholesome states except the defilements. These dhammas are associated with defilements but not defilements. Dhammas dissociated from defilements are not to be called defilements and associated with defilements, nor associated with defilements but not defilements.
^1-1578

Which dhammas are dissociated from defilements but defiling? Wholesome, resultant, and functional indeterminate states in the three planes; and all material phenomena. These dhammas are dissociated from defilements but defiling.
^1-1579

Which dhammas are dissociated from defilements and undefiling? The four supramundane paths, the four fruits of recluseship, and Nibbana. These dhammas are dissociated from defilements and undefiling. Dhammas associated with defilements are not to be called dissociated from defilements and defiling, nor dissociated from defilements and undefiling.
^1-1580

##### The Pair of Eradication ^1-4-2-13-0

Which dhammas are to be abandoned by seeing? The four mind-moments associated with wrong view, and the mind-moment accompanied by doubt. These dhammas are to be abandoned by seeing. The four mind-moments accompanied by greed dissociated from wrong view, and the two mind-moments accompanied by displeasure. These dhammas may or may not be abandoned by seeing.
^1-1581

Which dhammas are not to be abandoned by seeing? The mind-moment accompanied by restlessness; wholesome states in the four planes; resultant states in the four planes; functional indeterminate states in the three planes; material phenomena; and Nibbana. These dhammas are not to be abandoned by seeing.
^1-1582

Which phenomena are to be abandoned by development? The mind-state accompanied by restlessness – these phenomena are to be abandoned by development. The four mind-states accompanied by greed disconnected from wrong view, and the two mind-states accompanied by displeasure – these phenomena are sometimes to be abandoned by development, sometimes not to be abandoned by development.
^1-1583

Which phenomena are not to be abandoned by development? The four mind-states accompanied by wrong view, the mind-state accompanied by doubt, wholesome consciousness in the four planes, resultant consciousness in the four planes, functional consciousness in the three planes, materiality, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are not to be abandoned by development.
^1-1584

Which phenomena have causes to be abandoned by insight? The four mind-states accompanied by wrong view, the mind-state accompanied by doubt, excluding the delusion arising in them – these phenomena have causes to be abandoned by insight. The four mind-states accompanied by greed disconnected from wrong view, and the two mind-states accompanied by displeasure – these phenomena are sometimes having causes to be abandoned by insight, sometimes not having causes to be abandoned by insight.
^1-1585

Which phenomena do not have causes to be abandoned by insight? The delusion accompanied by doubt, the mind-state accompanied by restlessness, wholesome consciousness in the four planes, resultant consciousness in the four planes, functional consciousness in the three planes, materiality, and Nibbāna – these phenomena do not have causes to be abandoned by insight.
^1-1586

Which phenomena have causes to be abandoned by development? The mind-state accompanied by restlessness, excluding the delusion arising in it – these phenomena have causes to be abandoned by development. The four mind-states accompanied by greed disconnected from wrong view, and the two mind-states accompanied by displeasure – these phenomena are sometimes having causes to be abandoned by development, sometimes not having causes to be abandoned by development.
^1-1587

Which phenomena do not have causes to be abandoned by development? The four mind-states accompanied by wrong view, the mind-state accompanied by doubt, the delusion accompanied by restlessness, wholesome consciousness in the four planes, resultant consciousness in the four planes, functional consciousness in the three planes, materiality, and Nibbāna – these phenomena do not have causes to be abandoned by development.
^1-1588

Which phenomena are accompanied by initial application? Wholesome sense-sphere consciousness, unwholesome consciousness, eleven resultant consciousnesses of wholesome sense-sphere kamma, two resultant consciousnesses of unwholesome kamma, eleven functional consciousnesses, the first fine-material jhāna (wholesome, resultant, and functional), and the first supramundane jhāna (wholesome and resultant), excluding the initial application itself arising in them – these phenomena are accompanied by initial application.
^1-1589

Which phenomena are not accompanied by initial application? The ten five-sense consciousnesses, the third and fourth fine-material jhānas (wholesome, resultant, and functional), the four immaterial-sphere consciousnesses (wholesome, resultant, and functional), the third and fourth supramundane jhānas (wholesome and resultant), initial application itself, materiality, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are not accompanied by initial application.
^1-1590

Which phenomena are accompanied by sustained application? Wholesome sense-sphere consciousness, unwholesome consciousness, eleven resultant consciousnesses of wholesome sense-sphere kamma, two resultant consciousnesses of unwholesome kamma, eleven functional consciousnesses, the first and second fine-material jhānas (wholesome, resultant, and functional), and the first and second supramundane jhānas (wholesome and resultant), excluding the sustained application itself arising in them – these phenomena are accompanied by sustained application.
^1-1591

Which phenomena are not accompanied by sustained application? The ten five-sense consciousnesses, the third and fourth fine-material jhānas (wholesome, resultant, and functional), the four immaterial-sphere consciousnesses (wholesome, resultant, and functional), the third and fourth supramundane jhānas (wholesome and resultant), sustained application itself, materiality, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are not accompanied by sustained application.
^1-1592

Which phenomena are accompanied by rapture? Four sense-sphere wholesome consciousnesses accompanied by joy, four unwholesome consciousnesses, five resultant sense-sphere wholesome consciousnesses, five functional consciousnesses, the second and third fine-material jhānas (wholesome, resultant, and functional types), the second and third supramundane jhānas (wholesome and resultant types), excluding rapture itself that arises therein – these phenomena are accompanied by rapture.
^1-1593

Which phenomena are not accompanied by rapture? Four sense-sphere wholesome consciousnesses accompanied by equanimity, eight unwholesome consciousnesses, eleven resultant sense-sphere wholesome consciousnesses, seven resultant unwholesome consciousnesses, six functional consciousnesses, the fourth fine-material jhānas (wholesome, resultant, and functional types), the four immaterial consciousnesses (wholesome, resultant, and functional types), the fourth supramundane jhānas (wholesome and resultant types), rapture itself, all material phenomena, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are not accompanied by rapture.
^1-1594

Which phenomena are associated with rapture? Four sense-sphere wholesome consciousnesses accompanied by joy, four unwholesome consciousnesses, five resultant sense-sphere wholesome consciousnesses, five functional consciousnesses, the second and third fine-material jhānas (wholesome, resultant, and functional types), the second and third supramundane jhānas (wholesome and resultant types), excluding rapture itself that arises therein – these phenomena are associated with rapture.
^1-1595

Which phenomena are not associated with rapture? Four sense-sphere wholesome consciousnesses accompanied by equanimity, eight unwholesome consciousnesses, eleven resultant sense-sphere wholesome consciousnesses, seven resultant unwholesome consciousnesses, six functional consciousnesses, the fourth fine-material jhānas (wholesome, resultant, and functional types), the four immaterial consciousnesses (wholesome, resultant, and functional types), the fourth supramundane jhānas (wholesome and resultant types), rapture itself, all material phenomena, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are not associated with rapture.
^1-1596

Which phenomena are associated with happiness? Four sense-sphere wholesome consciousnesses accompanied by joy, four unwholesome consciousnesses, six resultant sense-sphere wholesome consciousnesses, five functional consciousnesses, the third and fourth fine-material jhānas (wholesome, resultant, and functional types), the third and fourth supramundane jhānas (wholesome and resultant types), excluding happiness itself that arises therein – these phenomena are associated with happiness.
^1-1597

Which phenomena are not associated with happiness? Four sense-sphere wholesome consciousnesses accompanied by equanimity, eight unwholesome consciousnesses, ten resultant sense-sphere wholesome consciousnesses, seven resultant unwholesome consciousnesses, six functional consciousnesses, the fourth fine-material jhāna (wholesome, resultant, and functional types), the four immaterial consciousnesses (wholesome, resultant, and functional types), the fourth supramundane jhāna (wholesome and resultant types), happiness itself, all material phenomena, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are not associated with happiness.
^1-1598

Which phenomena are associated with equanimity? Four sense-sphere wholesome consciousnesses accompanied by equanimity, six unwholesome consciousnesses, ten resultant sense-sphere wholesome consciousnesses, six resultant unwholesome consciousnesses, six functional consciousnesses, the fourth fine-material jhāna (wholesome, resultant, and functional types), the four immaterial consciousnesses (wholesome, resultant, and functional types), the fourth supramundane jhāna (wholesome and resultant types), excluding equanimity itself that arises therein – these phenomena are associated with equanimity.
^1-1599

Which phenomena are not associated with equanimity? Four sense-sphere wholesome consciousnesses accompanied by joy, six unwholesome consciousnesses, six resultant sense-sphere wholesome consciousnesses, one resultant unwholesome consciousness, five functional consciousnesses, the third and fourth fine-material jhānas (wholesome, resultant, and functional types), the third and fourth supramundane jhānas (wholesome and resultant types), equanimity itself, all material phenomena, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are not associated with equanimity.
^1-1600

Which phenomena belong to the sense-sphere? All sense-sphere wholesome consciousnesses, all unwholesome consciousnesses, all resultant sense-sphere consciousnesses, all functional sense-sphere consciousnesses, and all material phenomena – these phenomena belong to the sense-sphere.
^1-1601

Which phenomena do not belong to the sense-sphere? All fine-material sphere phenomena, all immaterial sphere phenomena, and all unincluded phenomena – these phenomena do not belong to the sense-sphere.
^1-1602

Which phenomena are fine-material sphere? The fourfold and fivefold fine-material absorptions, whether wholesome, resultant, or functional – these phenomena are fine-material sphere.
^1-1603

Which phenomena are not fine-material sphere? Sense-sphere, immaterial-sphere, and unincluded phenomena – these phenomena are not fine-material sphere.
^1-1604

Which phenomena are immaterial sphere? The four immaterial attainments, whether wholesome, resultant, or functional – these phenomena are immaterial sphere.
^1-1605

Which phenomena are not immaterial sphere? Sense-sphere, fine-material sphere, and unincluded phenomena – these phenomena are not immaterial sphere.
^1-1606

Which phenomena are included? Wholesome, unwholesome, resultant, and functional indeterminate phenomena in the three planes, and all matter – these phenomena are included.
^1-1607

Which phenomena are unincluded? The four transcendent paths, the four transcendent fruits, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are unincluded.
^1-1608

Which phenomena lead to liberation? The four transcendent paths – these phenomena lead to liberation.
^1-1609

Which phenomena do not lead to liberation? Wholesome and unwholesome phenomena in the three planes, resultant phenomena in the four planes, functional indeterminate phenomena in the three planes, matter, and Nibbāna – these phenomena do not lead to liberation.
^1-1610

Which phenomena are fixed? The four consciousnesses associated with wrong view and the two consciousnesses accompanied by displeasure – these phenomena are sometimes fixed, sometimes unfixed. The four transcendent paths – these phenomena are fixed.
^1-1611

Which phenomena are unfixed? The four consciousnesses rooted in greed dissociated from wrong view, the consciousness accompanied by doubt, the consciousness accompanied by restlessness, wholesome phenomena in the three planes, resultant phenomena in the four planes, functional indeterminate phenomena in the three planes, matter, and Nibbāna – these phenomena are unfixed.
^1-1612

Which phenomena are with superior? Wholesome, unwholesome, resultant, and functional indeterminate phenomena in the three planes, and all matter 
– these phenomena are with superior.
^1-1613

Which phenomena are without superior? The four supramundane paths, the four fruitions of recluseship, and Nibbāna 
– these phenomena are without superior.
^1-1614

Which phenomena are with refuge/defilement? The twelve unwholesome consciousness-arisings 
– these phenomena are with refuge/defilement.
^1-1615

Which phenomena are without refuge/defilement? Wholesome and resultant phenomena in the four planes, functional indeterminate phenomena in the three planes, matter, and Nibbāna 
– these phenomena are without refuge/defilement.
The summary of meanings is concluded.
The treatise Dhammasaṅgaṇī is concluded.
^1-1616
